## Archived: 2026-06-30 ~10:30 — refined-transcript REAL root cause found + fixed; owner testing 2h video

> ⚡ **STATUS 2026-06-30 ~10:30 (Jarvis — refined-transcript REAL root cause found + fixed; owner testing 2h video. READ FIRST.)** Branch `qa-fixes`, latest commit drops the refined duration cap.
>
> **refinedTranscript EMPTY — REAL root cause (after 4 wrong/partial fixes):** `shouldGenerateRefinedTranscript` (generate-ai.ts:305) is called with `videoDurationSeconds = getVideoDuration(transcript.segments)` — derived from the TRANSCRIPT SEGMENTS, not the video's `duration`. Old transcripts have HALLUCINATED trailing VTT cues (e.g. a 7min video with a 01:02:20 cue), so getVideoDuration returns a value > the 12-min (`MAX_REFINED_TRANSCRIPT_AUTO_SECONDS=720`) cap → `includeRefinedTranscript=false` → the whole refined path (incl. my fallback) is SKIPPED → Refined empty + the workflow finishes in ~10s. Also the cap was wrong anyway: owner records up-to-2h lessons. **FIX: `shouldGenerateRefinedTranscript` now returns `transcriptCharCount > 0` (no duration gate).** Combined with the earlier per-chunk raw-text FALLBACK + the empty-response finishReason logging, the Refined tab should now populate for ALL videos. **VERIFY NEXT: re-gen vqc3v00cqpm8d6k → refinedTranscript.chapters > 0.** (Earlier fixes that did NOT crack it but are committed + fine: char-cap removal, maxOutputTokens 65536, raw fallback.) LESSON (generalizable): never gate a feature on a fragile DERIVED proxy (duration-from-hallucinated-segments); gate on the real requirement (a transcript exists).
>
> **2 NEW owner reports while testing a 2h video (NEITHER is my regression — verified):** (1) **black video cards / no thumbnail** — `screen-capture.jpg` (captured CLIENT-side by the recorder during recording, `useWebRecorder.ts`) is MISSING in S3 for some videos (thumbnail API: 1min video=200, 10min video=404 "No thumbnail found"). The 2h video is black because its upload stalled before finishing. My recorder changes were i18n-only; thumbnail capture/gen untouched. (2) **2h upload stalled at 97%** — the in-browser multipart uploader (`instant-mp4-uploader.ts`, MIN_PART_SIZE=5MB, MAX_PART_UPLOAD_ATTEMPTS=3, has stall-detection) gave up on a late part/finalize after retries. `actions/video/upload.ts` UNCHANGED by me. This is large-file/browser-upload fragility, pre-existing. Possible mitigation (needs owner re-test, can't repro a 2h upload here): raise MAX_PART_UPLOAD_ATTEMPTS 3→6 + longer stall timeout, or record shorter segments.
>
> **refinedTranscript EMPTY root cause (likely):** `callAiApi` in `apps/web/workflows/generate-ai.ts` used `maxOutputTokens: 8192` for BOTH the summary JSON call AND the refined-transcript (json:false) call. The refined call outputs the FULL cleaned transcript (~as long as the input) → 8192 truncated/dropped it → empty content → `generateRefinedTranscriptSingle` returns `chapters: []` (line ~861). FIX: `maxOutputTokens: json ? 8192 : 65536` (give the long free-text cleaning call the model's full output room). **VERIFY NEXT: deploy → re-gen the 7min (vqc3v00cqpm8d6k) + 10min (xqmcnsvq66m5yt6) videos (clear summary+chapters via DB → retry-ai) → check `aiSummary.refinedTranscript.chapters > 0`.** If still empty on a SHORT video, the cap wasn't the cause — instrument the refined call's finishReason. (LESSON, generalizable: a variable-length 'transform the whole document' LLM call needs an output-token cap sized for the FULL output; a cap tuned for a short structured response silently truncates/empties long free-text.)

## Archived: history — AI ANALYSIS re-gen issue + SECURITY REVIEW + refinedTranscript fix

> 🔴 **(history) AI ANALYSIS re-gen issue — clarified above (was: "broken for every video").** Re-triggering AI via `POST /api/videos/:id/retry-ai` returns 200 "started inline" but the inline `generateAiWorkflow(...).catch()` THROWS early → status goes to ERROR within ~10s, aiSummary unchanged. Confirmed on BOTH a 63s (single-chunk) AND a 7-10min video → NOT caused by the refinedTranscript fix; generation is broken GENERALLY. The error was only `console.error`'d server-side (no Railway log access here). **Deployed `430d45e`** which persists the real error to `metadata.aiGenerationError`; NEXT: once live, reset a video's status + re-trigger + read `aiGenerationError` from the DB (via the public MySQL proxy) to get the actual root cause, then fix. (Initial-upload generation produced the existing summaries; re-gen now fails — something in the current deploy or environment, e.g. transcript-fetch, Gemini key, or AI budget exhausted — `withCostGuard`.)
>
> 🔴 **SECURITY REVIEW (Workflow, this session's diff) — 3 confirmed findings to FIX (all from the Trash/soft-delete work):**
>   1. **HIGH — `permanentlyDelete` authz broken** (`packages/web-backend/src/Videos/index.ts` ~L229-256): `policy.isOwner` uses `repo.getById()` which filters out soft-deleted rows, but permanentlyDelete fetches via `getByIdIncludingDeleted()` → the policy ALWAYS denies, so even the owner can't purge their own trashed video (and the 7-day purge cron fails). Fix: add `isOwnerIncludingDeleted` policy variant (or pass the already-fetched video to the check).
>   2. **MEDIUM — storage access before authz** (same file, `storage.getAccessForVideo(video)` runs before `policy.isOwner`): reorder so the ownership check happens first (defense-in-depth / existence-probe).
>   3. **HIGH — `/api/videos/[videoId]/generate/route.ts:31` missing `isNull(videos.deletedAt)` guard** → AI/transcription can be triggered on a soft-deleted video (other endpoints have the guard; this one forgot it). Add the guard.
>
> ✅ **refinedTranscript FIX = committed `1c2b93a` + DEPLOYED + gate-green (typecheck PASS, vitest 549/20).** Root cause (Workflow diagnosis, 2 agents high-confidence): `shouldGenerateRefinedTranscript` gated on `transcriptCharCount <= MAX_REFINED_TRANSCRIPT_AUTO_CHARS` (12000) → long videos exceeded it so refined was NEVER requested; the refined transcript is generated SEPARATELY per chunk (not in the summary token budget) so total length is irrelevant — now gated on DURATION only. Also: per-chunk refined Gemini call now wrapped in try/catch (one failing chunk no longer collapses refinedChapters to []); dropped the `&& refinedChapters.length>0` guard; unit test updated to assert duration-gating.
>   - ⚠️ **END-TO-END VERIFY BLOCKED — could NOT confirm on an existing video.** Authenticated re-gen test (admin login OK, `POST /api/videos/:id/retry-ai` → 200 "AI generation started inline") but the generation NEVER PROGRESSED: aiGenerationStatus stayed at my reset value, aiSummary unchanged, no aiGenerationError logged. **The inline fire-and-forget generation (`generateAiWorkflow(payload).catch()` — Workflow Dev Kit disabled) does not reliably execute when triggered via the HTTP retry endpoint** (likely the promise is dropped after the response returns — a PRE-EXISTING issue, not caused by this fix). So existing videos can't be BACKFILLED via the retry button until that trigger is fixed. (I restored the test video's status to COMPLETE.) **The fix WILL apply to NEWLY recorded/uploaded videos** (their AI runs inside the transcribe workflow). **TO VERIFY: record/upload a NEW >30min-ish video and check its refined transcript populates — OR fix the HTTP re-gen trigger (needs Railway server logs, owner-accessible).**
>
> 🔴 **(history) NEW BUG FOUND — `refinedTranscript` EMPTY for long videos (now fixed above).** DB evidence: 63s video→1 refined chapter; **7min→0; 10min→0**. Title/summary/chapters/topics all populate; the readable "refined transcript" (cleaned, chaptered paragraphs, `aiSummary.refinedTranscript.chapters`) is empty on longer videos, so viewers see only raw timestamped VTT cues = "not comfortable". Code: `apps/web/workflows/generate-ai.ts` — refinedTranscript is generated SEPARATELY (8192-token cap forces it out of the summary call), chunk-by-chunk; the per-chunk refined calls appear to silently return empty/fail for multi-chunk (long) videos. **NEXT: investigate the per-chunk refined-generation failure in generate-ai.ts (~L300-340 multi-chunk merge + L749-816 single-chunk path), fix, then re-generate AI for existing videos (have DB + admin access).**

HEAD now **`512269c`, 10 commits local-unpushed**. Pattern: Opus authored uz/ru (quality), wiring delegated. **LESSON (big): for a multi-file mechanical sweep use a Workflow PARALLEL fan-out (one agent per file — share's 24 files took 2.5min) — do NOT use subagent_type:"claude" recursive chains, they go one file at a time and burned ~1M+ tokens. Also: when a wired function gains a `t` param, its unit-test callers + other callers (e.g. CapVideoPlayer) must be updated — typecheck catches it.**
>
> **#5 meeting-record BUG-2/3/5 — ASSESSED (code-evidenced, 2026-06-30), conclusion = do NOT blind-fix; needs owner's live Meet test:**
>   - **BUG-3 (nextPartNumber race) = NOT A LIVE BUG.** `handleChunk` is serialized via `enqueueRecorder` (`apps/browser-extension/src/background/sw.ts:287,750`) — chunks run one-at-a-time, so the race can't occur (the `dfc6ef8` serialization already defends it). **No fix needed.**
>   - **BUG-2 (webm bytes under `result.mp4` key, `upload.ts:320` contentType=video/webm + `:322` subpath=result.mp4):** the contentType IS correctly `video/webm`; only the object KEY is named `.mp4`. The extension recorder REQUIRES Chromium, so the owner's users are on Chrome where webm plays fine — the "corrupt on some browsers" is a Safari type-sniff edge. Changing the key needs coordinated backend changes (`playlist/route.ts` serves `result.mp4` at L282/348; `transcribe.ts` falls back to raw-upload.webm) and a live Meet test. **Risk > value; do NOT change blind.**
>   - **BUG-5 (weak-net recovery):** a retry queue exists (`addToRetryQueue` in upload.ts); only a user-facing recovery path/UX is missing. Addable but needs a live weak-network Meet test to verify.
>   - **Verdict: the meeting-record pipeline is fragile + un-testable here (needs a real Google Meet recording + extension rebuild + side-load). Blind-shipping concurrency/upload changes risks breaking ALL meeting recordings. Hand to owner: do a live Meet recording, confirm whether BUG-2 actually affects playback (likely not on Chrome) and whether weak-net recovery is needed, THEN fix with the test in the loop.**
>
> **REMAINING = ALL OWNER-GATED:** (a) PUSH decision — 10 commits unpushed = Railway deploy + live migration 0035 (`videos.deletedAt`, safe additive) + Trash purge-cron needs a schedule; (b) #7 live RU/EN transcript + cost-confirm verify (needs a non-Uzbek video); (c) live i18n check (switch uz/ru/en, confirm layouts in light+dark); (d) #5 live Meet test (above); (e) #9 FINAL `/code-review ultra` + `/security-review` + `qa-6-step` (owner-triggered, billed).
>
> **`/goalkeeper` skill INSTALLED** (`~/.claude/skills/goalkeeper/`, backup at `~/.claude/skills.bak-20260630`) — autonomous build/verify loop. Good for test-backed code goals; NOT used for i18n (fuzzy "done") nor for #5 (needs a live Meet test it can't run). Note: it does `git reset` on failed rounds → only run it on a CLEAN tree, never alongside other uncommitted edits.
>
> **Delegation reality:** `subagent_type:"claude"` recursively spawns sub-agent CHAINS (recorder took ~8 chained agents, ~500k tokens, left transient broken intermediate files) but DOES converge. Always: LF-normalize touched files (`sed -i 's/\r$//'`) + ROOT typecheck + vitest + review uz quality before commit. Old "Cap/cap" brand can leak into translations — grep + fix.
>
> **#6 i18n uz/ru/en — SCOPE = core user-facing screens only (owner-approved: dashboard nav, share-page, settings, auth, recorder). NOT all 363 components.** Pattern: next-intl, `messages/{en,uz,ru}.json` namespaces, `useTranslations("ns")`; uz/ru authored by Opus (quality), .tsx wiring delegated to Sonnet + Opus-reviewed + gated.

> **Owner Qs answered this session (cost):** (1) "users save to their own Google Drive to make it free?" → app ALREADY has ORG-level Google Drive storage (Settings→Org→Integrations, provider s3|googleDrive); per-USER Drive is a bad fit (streaming/tokens/control). R2 free tier (zero egress) already ~free; real cost = Railway. (2) "save Gemini cost without quality loss?" → chat already RAG-optimized + once-only + gated. **INVESTIGATED post-Trash — REALITY (corrects my earlier 30-50% estimate, which was WRONG):** (a) combine calls = **ALREADY DONE** — `generateWithAi`/`generateSingleChunk` produces title+summary+chapters+refined-transcript in ONE Gemini call. (b) context caching = **no benefit** — generation is one-shot (no repeated context), chat already RAG; Gemini 2.5 implicit caching is automatic/free. So #1+#2 need NO work — AI is already optimal. (c) Batch API (50% cheaper) = **ADVISED AGAINST** (Jarvis judgment): async (minutes-hours delay) conflicts with the fast/progressive UX, big rearchitecture, saves only ~$0.05/video = net-negative. Owner had said "admin toggle"; flagged my recommendation to skip. NOT building unless owner re-confirms. Transcription = quality floor, untouched.

(3) **Dark-toggle instant** (Test A live-verified PASS). *Verify still pending:* cost-dialog live-confirm (nested-agent flakiness + private-video `/s/` route blocks owner-view) and RU/EN transcript not tested on a non-Uzbek video.
>
> **AI cost (told owner):** `gemini-2.5-flash` only ($0.10/M in, $0.40/M out; embeddings free) -> ~$0.10 for a 2hr video. Caps $100/mo + $20/video (`ai-cost-guard.ts`; Org-Settings configurable; backstop only — main control = the new confirm dialog). **Long-video AI analysis is UNRELIABLE / FAILS** (R2 ground-truth: the real long video `xqmcnsvq66m5yt6` 213MB = transcriptionStatus **ERROR**, AI never ran. An earlier "works" claim was WRONG — it was based on `fg7dpjtqz45qchg` which the owner has since DELETED, so it no longer exists). **ROOT CAUSE (autoresearch:debug, code-confirmed):** the chunk loop in `apps/web/workflows/transcribe.ts` had NO partial-failure tolerance — if any ONE of N chunks failed after 2 retries it threw, the outer catch marked the WHOLE video ERROR and dropped every successful chunk; a 2hr video = 8 chunks = ~8x failure surface. **FIX applied (uncommitted/gating):** fault-tolerant chunk loop (a failed chunk -> keep a gap + continue; only fail if ALL chunks fail) + chunk retries 2->4. **ALSO FOUND (separate bug, not yet fixed):** hallucinated trailing VTT cues even on short videos (a 55s video has a `01:02:20` cue) -> needs cue-vs-audio-duration validation. **Verify the fix** by re-running transcription on `xqmcnsvq66m5yt6` after deploy.
>
> **(!) COMMITTED LOCAL, NOT DEPLOYED — DO NOT PUSH AS-IS (`1853850`):** server video compression wired into `processVideoWorkflow` (optimizeRemoteVideoToMp4, CRF23). SAFETY TOO WEAK to ship: it OVERWRITES `result.mp4` after only an `fs.stat`>100KB check — NO playability/duration verify -> a bad-but-large re-encode could DESTROY the original; + inline re-encode blocks processing minutes on long videos. FIX before deploy: verify playable + duration within ~2% & keep original until verified — OR safer path = just lower the recording bitrate. Owner approved compression IF quality stays good + free.
>
> **AGREED, NOT STARTED:** (1) **Trash / soft-delete with 7-day retention + restore** — TODAY delete is HARD: `Videos.delete` purges the DB row + ALL S3 files immediately, NO recovery/trash. (2) Full **UI i18n uz/ru/en** (#5b — some hardcoded strings remain). Storage: owner on **10 GB R2 free tier**; quota ENFORCED (upload blocked when full — `checkUploadQuota`).
>
> **RECORDING AUDIT (2026-06-29):** Instructions = in-browser recorder (mostly OK). **Meeting Recordings = a SEPARATE browser-EXTENSION path** (Google Meet, `source.context='meeting'`) with REAL bugs: BUG-2 (HIGH) extension stores webm bytes under the `result.mp4` key (`apps/web/.../upload.ts:143,320,500`) -> corrupt/unplayable on some browsers; BUG-3 (HIGH) stale `nextPartNumber` race -> duplicate parts on fast networks; BUG-4 (MED) `size:0` parts -> broken progress; BUG-5 (HIGH, =DATA365_REMAINING #3) extension weak-net recovery has no user path; BUG-7 (MED) MP4 blob named `.webm` may break Safari convert. Recording bitrate IS capped at 600kbps (NOT uncompressed - earlier claim corrected). Cap-wordmark appears already fixed. Several need a LIVE meeting-recording test to confirm.
>
> **LONG-VIDEO FIX DEPLOYED (`470a784` pushed -> Railway):** fault-tolerant chunk loop + retries 2->4; the unsafe compression commit `1853850` was REVERTED (in `470a784`) to keep the branch deployable. **VERIFY FIRST next session:** re-run transcription on `xqmcnsvq66m5yt6` (213MB, currently ERROR) -> should now COMPLETE.

**FOURTH REVIEW (delta `6faf667..0f435a4`) — 5 more, and it named the structural truth the previous rounds kept working around.** `extractAudioChunksFromUrl` (`lib/audio-extract.ts:414`) runs the remote download, a full `silencedetect` decode and every per-chunk transcode inside ONE uninterrupted await with no progress callback, so there is NO code boundary inside it to stamp — every "heartbeat at point X" scheme keeps a blind window as long as the slowest await, and the round-4 test could not fail on it because it asserts a stamp downstream of the slow work. Round 5 was therefore told to stop stamping boundaries: liveness means THE PROCESS IS RUNNING, so stamp it from a periodic timer started at claim and cleared in a `finally`. Also open: the quota ladder's 12-attempt budget is unreachable (the 4 h paid flip is evaluated first, so a >4 h free-quota outage still bills a cheap job — 3 free kicks, then paid, rungs 4–12 dead code); the flip branch itself has no budget, so an org over its AI budget spins forever with no ERROR and no alert; the title patterns now false-positive on human titles containing a date or a time (`2026-08-10 team sync.mp4`, `Sprint review 10:30`); and the new quota-park write reintroduced the JS spread that can erase a billed `aiBatchJobs`. Confirmed CLEAN: the `COALESCE(..., '')` SQL arm really selects marker-less rows on MySQL (no writer produces JSON null), the shared quota counter cannot starve or double-kick, the `autoRecoverAttempts` refund is safe.

**HONEST STANDING ASSESSMENT, written for whoever resumes this: 5 build rounds, 4 blind reviews, 20 defects, and every round's fix created a new one.** Green gates were green at every single round. Two of the defect families keep recurring in new shapes — the money one (cheap silently becoming paid, reintroduced three separate ways) and the rename one (reversed twice, now false-positive instead of false-negative). Before the next round is trusted, consider whether the recovery cron plus workflow-liveness pair is worth a deliberate redesign rather than another patch; that is a judgement for the owner's working session, not something to start unattended.

**ROUND 5 (committed on `qa-fixes` after `0f435a4`) — all five findings closed; the liveness mechanism finally changed shape instead of moving a number.** (1) `aiProgressAt` is now stamped by a 5-min `setInterval` started after the claim and cleared in a `finally` on every exit path (success, no-audio, quota-park throw, crash) — the timer dies with the process, so a killed container stops stamping automatically; ALL boundary stamps were removed (single liveness owner). 5 min vs the 60-min threshold = 12× margin. (2) The quota-parked ladder's comment/test are now HONEST: the 4 h paid flip (the UI's own "4 soatda avtomatik tezlashadi" promise) is the ceiling — ~3 free re-kicks fit inside it; changing that ceiling is an owner decision, deliberately not taken unattended. (3) The paid flip has a bounded budget (`paidFlipAttempts`, max 3): failures are counted server-side, and at the bound the row goes terminal ERROR + ops alert instead of spinning forever on an over-budget org. (4) Machine-title verdicts now require the timestamp to BE essentially the whole title — `2026-08-10 team sync` and `Sprint review 10:30` are protected, `Monday, 6h59m PM (1)` still renames. (5) The quota-park write is a server-side `JSON_SET`/`JSON_REMOVE`, so it can no longer erase a billed `aiBatchJobs`. RED proof: 3/3 pinned test files fail with round-5 sources stashed. A mid-round network cutoff produced a stale "does not compile" report — refuted against the tree (all symbols wired), gates re-run fresh.

**ROUND 6 (FINAL, committed after `fae7de1`) — the OWNER redefined "sekin" and the judge ordered a narrow redesign + FREEZE.** Owner's words: "imkon qadar bepuldan foydalanadi, u tugasa qaytguncha pullikdan foydalanadi" — so cheap now = free first, and on ANY free failure it continues IMMEDIATELY on the discounted Batch queue (never parks for hours, never touches the full-price sync call; the last such path — non-quota free failures falling through to paid — is closed and pinned). The automatic 4h paid flip for TRANSCRIPTION is REMOVED along with its UI promise; termination is now guaranteed by: the Batch collector (rows with a Batch), the bounded free ladder ending in terminal ERROR(QUOTA)+alert (parked rows without one), and bounded stale re-kicks ending in ERROR+alert (crashed rows). The generation-phase 4h flip REMAINS (generation has no discounted route, so paid-sync IS its cheapest paid path — consistent with the owner's rule). Structural fix: `lib/gemini-paid-key.ts` is now the ONLY reader of the paid key on the AI path — every use names its operation/mode/route, cheap+sync THROWS, and a source-scan test fails the build on any direct `.GEMINI_API_KEY` read outside the gate. `isMachineFormattedTitle` deleted (provenance `titleAutoGenerated` only; legacy rows keep the template-regex fallback; consequence accepted: an imported file with a machine-made NAME will not auto-rename). All three locales rewritten to the new truth (no "4 soatda tezlashadi" anywhere); the panel now distinguishes "discounted paid queue running" from "waiting for free quota" via a plumbed aiBatchPending flag. FREEZE is in effect: no new branches/ladders/counters in recover-stale-ai-jobs.

**THE OWNER WAS ASKED TO JUDGE THE APPROACH, AND AN INDEPENDENT JUDGE (not the author, not any of the five reviewers) RULED: narrow REDESIGN, then FREEZE.** Its finding, verified in the code before acting: the money defect was never a race, a snapshot or a heartbeat — it was **the absence of a boundary**. `serverEnv().GEMINI_API_KEY` was ambiently in scope at ~11 sites, so every error path's default was paid, and **nothing anywhere asserted "a cheap job must not bill the full-price path"** — which is exactly why `tsc`, ~1300 tests and `next build` caught 0 of 20 defects across five rounds. A jobs-table rewrite (3-5 days) would not have prevented a single one of the three money reintroductions; the capability gate does. Judge's blunt count: of the 20 defects, roughly **3 were pre-existing user-facing problems** — the rest were the fix eating its own tail. Its own strongest counter-argument is recorded too: a lease-based jobs table would structurally have prevented more than it admits, and "don't buy reliability you can't observe" is a bet on volume staying small.

**⭐ OWNER DECISION 2026-08-11 (a WHAT — do not re-litigate), in his words: _"imkon qadar bepuldan foydalanadi, u tugasa qaytguncha pullikdan foydalanadi"_.** Cheap = use free as much as possible; when free is exhausted, continue on the paid path until free returns. **He does not want waiting** — which overturned the premise rounds 2–5 were all built on. Asked how to finish, he said _"o'ylab qaror qil, sifatli bo'lsin eng muhimi"_ (my call, quality first) → the judge's narrow redesign, not the rewrite.

**ROUND 6 = FINAL (`5d3cb98`), and the FREEZE is now in force.** Removed: the automatic 4 h cheap→full-price flip for transcription and its counters; the last cheap→full-price fallthrough (a NON-quota free failure used to drop straight to the full-price sync call — the same money defect in its fourth shape, still live in prod today); `isMachineFormattedTitle` entirely; every "4 soatda avtomatik tezlashadi" promise in all three locales. Added: `lib/gemini-paid-key.ts` as the SOLE reader of the paid key (11 sites converted, every use names operation/mode/route, `transcription+cheap+sync` throws, and a source-scan test fails on any direct `.GEMINI_API_KEY` read anywhere under lib/workflows/app/actions/hooks); the invariant "a cheap job never bills the full-price path" as a runtime throw plus behaviour pins; `aiBatchPending` plumbing so the panel distinguishes "discounted queue running" from "waiting for free quota" instead of claiming a queue that may not exist. **Kept deliberately:** the round-5 timer heartbeat, and the GENERATION-phase 4 h flip (generation has no discounted route, so paid-sync is its cheapest paid path under the owner's rule — stated, not hidden). **Termination without the flip:** pending Batch → the collector; quota-parked without one → the bounded free ladder ending in terminal ERROR(QUOTA) + ops alert; crashed → bounded stale re-kicks ending in ERROR + ops alert. No automatic cheap→full-price rescue exists anywhere; the owner's manual "continue on paid" button is the only such path.

Two families recurred in new forms: the money one (three reintroductions) and the auto-rename one (reversed twice, then deleted rather than tuned a third time).

**ROUND 7 (the final pre-ship review returned DO-NOT-SHIP on `fae7de1..5d3cb98`; all five findings fixed and committed).** (1) The Batch↔free cycle is BOUNDED: each cheap Batch submit books a per-chunk counter (a different chunk submitting = progress = reset); at 3 submits for the same chunk it stops billing — a multi-chunk video degrades that chunk to a transcript gap and still completes, a single-chunk video goes terminal ERROR — with an ops alert either way. This restores the terminator the removed 4h flip used to provide WITHOUT any automatic escalation to full price (the separation the round-6 brief itself missed: the owner's "no long waits" never meant "no terminator"). (2) Gap-fill never rethrows — a quota-wait mid-gap-fill was discarding a FULLY TRANSCRIBED video into a day of ladder deaths ending in ERROR; a failed span now simply stays a gap. (3) Owner-key-only deployments work in cheap mode: with no server key the Batch route cannot exist, so the video owner's own saved key on the sync call is the cheapest available paid path and is used; with a server key present cheap still always goes to Batch (pinned). (4) An unprocessable chunk (corrupt/safety-blocked) now degrades to a gap via the same bound instead of parking the whole video. (5) The paid-key scan catches bracket access + destructuring and scans `scripts/` too; the one script reading the var directly now goes through the gate (new "ops-script" authorization).

**THE PRE-SHIP REVIEW OF `5d3cb98` RETURNED DO-NOT-SHIP — and it was right.** Two regressions of the same class the whole sequence keeps producing, both introduced by round 6 itself: (a) removing the 4 h flip removed the Batch state's ONLY terminator, so a cheap job whose free path keeps failing looped Batch→free→Batch forever, submitting a NEW BILLED Batch and re-running the whole ffmpeg extract every cycle, while the panel showed "discounted queue is running" indefinitely; (b) round 6 widened "any free failure in cheap mode" into a quota-wait and applied it blindly to `fillTranscriptGaps`, where no Batch fallback exists — so a transient error during the OPTIONAL gap-fill of an already-fully-transcribed video threw the finished transcript away and ended in ERROR after a day of futile re-kicks. **Part of (a) is on the brief, recorded so the lesson survives: the owner's decision was "no long waits", NOT "no terminator", and my instruction to Fable did not separate the two.** Also found: the capability gate made the owner's OWN saved Gemini key unreachable in cheap mode (production has a server key — verified — so it does not bite today, but the client migration may well be owner-key-only), and the source-scan test guarding the whole design missed two read shapes and the `scripts/` directory that already contained a direct read. Confirmed clean by the same review: no direct paid-key read survives outside the gate, the manual "continue on paid" button and the Batch collector cannot trip the violation throw, `aiSpeedMode` is resolved once and threaded, `aiBatchPending` is derived from real metadata in both the API and the SSR fallback, and title provenance works in both directions.

**ROUND 7 (`3a8a029`) fixes all five.** The Batch↔free cycle is now bounded by `aiBatchChunkSubmits` keyed to `aiBatchChunkStartSec` (a DIFFERENT chunk submitting counts as progress and resets it); at 3 submits for the same chunk it stops billing and raises an ops alert — multi-chunk degrades that chunk to a transcript gap so the video still COMPLETES, single-chunk with a non-quota error goes terminal ERROR. `fillTranscriptGaps` never rethrows, so a finished transcript can no longer be discarded. `ownerKeyIsOnlyPaidPath` (cheap ∧ no server key ∧ owner key present) lets an owner-key-only deployment finish, while a deployment WITH a server key still always routes cheap to Batch. The scan now covers dot/bracket/destructuring reads and `scripts/`. **Gates re-run by the main session: `tsc -b --force` 0 · web suite 1340 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0 · diff 10 files +358/−60, `-w` delta 2 lines.**

**THE SHIP GATE ON `3a8a029` ALSO RETURNED DO-NOT-SHIP, and that is what forced the real decision.** Its findings: the "bounded" Batch cycle was not bounded (the counter keys on ONE chunk identity and every submit overwrites it, so two bad chunks reset each other forever — ~5 billed Batch submits and 8 full ffmpeg re-extracts per period, indefinitely); the cycle counters were cleared only by `saveTranscription`, so a video that died at cycle-exhausted could never be retried — the retry button changed nothing; and `"ops-script"` was an unguarded authorization that skipped every mode/route check.

**⭐ ROUND 8 = THE DECISION THAT SHOULD HAVE COME FIRST: DELETE, don't patch. Two corrections drove it, and the first one is MINE, recorded so it is never repeated.**
- **The invariant I had built was wrong.** I made Fable assert *"a cheap job must never bill the full-price path"* — my extrapolation from the judge's report, written before the owner stated his rule. His verbatim rule is *"imkon qadar bepuldan foydalanadi, u tugasa qaytguncha pullikdan foydalanadi"*: paying once free is exhausted is REQUESTED behaviour. The correct invariant is **"a cheap job never calls a paid API while free quota is still available"** — strictly weaker, and the stronger version is what manufactured three generations of machinery (park signalling, cycle counters, ladder terminals, owner-key special cases) to satisfy a rule nobody asked for.
- **The durable Batch machinery was the complexity generator** — submit → park → external collector → re-kick is the loop that produced every "state nobody owns". Deleted from the cheap transcription path. **Cheap is now: per chunk, try the free chain; if free is exhausted, make the SAME synchronous paid call and continue; the next chunk tries free again**, so the job returns to free the moment quota does. No parking, no collector coupling, no re-kick cycle — a cheap job terminates through the machinery a fast job already uses. Batch's ~2x discount applies only to overflow chunks and the whole monthly spend is $1.45, so it was worth cents against a day of defects.
- Gap-fill in cheap is now FREE-ONLY (a failed span stays a gap) — polish on an already-complete transcript should cost zero.

**`d3c3e30` — the FIRST round whose honest metric is negative: 20 files, +331/−656 (net −325), `-w` −325 too.** Gates re-run by the main session: `tsc -b --force` **0** · web suite **1338 passed / 26 skipped** · real `next build` **0**.

**MEASURED ON PROD BEFORE SHIPPING — the deletion strands nothing.** Rows carrying `aiBatchJobs`: **0**. Rows with `aiQuotaWaiting = true`: **0**. Rows in `PROCESSING`: **0**. Live library = 13 COMPLETE + 4 never-started. So no in-flight job and no legacy row depends on the deleted park/collector path; `poll-batch-jobs` is kept only to drain a legacy batch that, as of now, does not exist.

**THE SHIP GATE ON `d3c3e30` RETURNED DO-NOT-SHIP — and its top finding was MY brief's fault, for the second time.** Round 8 wrote `freeExhausted: true` as a hardcoded LITERAL rather than a classified fact, because my brief said *"if free is exhausted, make the paid call"* — which states WHEN, not HOW TO KNOW. So any free failure at all (a revoked/rotated free key, `400 API_KEY_INVALID`, a safety block, a malformed VTT) sent **every chunk of every cheap video to the paid API within seconds, with free quota fully intact** — the owner's exact reported defect, one abstraction lower, and pinned green by a test that asserted the bug ("free failure any reason → paid"). Two more: the quota-wait exemption in `shouldAutoRetry`/`withIncrementedAutoRecoverAttempts` survived while the ladder branch that bounded it was deleted, so a legacy parked row could re-kick forever; and the gate's `mode`/`route` were caller-DECLARED strings with three call sites hardcoding `mode: "fast"` — the value that disables the check. Long clean list, though: new-job termination, panel honesty, gap-fill free-only with FAST untouched, and every deletion confirmed to have zero remaining readers.

**ROUND 9 (`91390d6`) — the corrections, small diff (13 files, +212/−96).** `freeExhausted` is now a classified FACT: only `isQuotaLikeAiError` on the free failure, or the absence of any free key, sets it; every other cheap free failure rethrows into the chunk-retry → gap machinery. `authorizePaidGeminiUse` runs BEFORE any key is resolved, so the `ownerApiKey ?? gate` short-circuit can no longer skip the check and the owner's personal key obeys the identical rule; the gate refuses `freeExhausted: false` outright. `shouldCountStaleJobTowardDeath` is deleted so every row is counted. The authorization union is split: job-bound uses carry a DERIVED mode, job-less uses (translation/chat/ops-script/owner-continue-paid) have no mode field to falsify. The free-tier test now asserts the owner's rule, with a new pin that a broken free key produces ZERO paid calls. **Gates re-run by the main session: `tsc -b --force` 0 · web suite 1340 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.**

**⚠️ DISCLOSED SIDE EFFECT to watch after deploy:** cheap GENERATION quota-parks share the helpers whose exemption was deleted, so they are now bounded too (≤2 re-kicks → ERROR + alert), usually firing BEFORE their 4-hour paid flip. Bounded and visible, but a generation-phase behaviour change made as a side effect of a transcription fix — it is in the ship gate's brief.

**THE SHIP GATE ON `91390d6` RETURNED DO-NOT-SHIP, and the top finding is the TWIN — the blast-radius law this project's own memory warns about, skipped by me and by Fable.** Round 9 hardened `workflows/transcribe.ts` and never looked at its copy-pasted sibling: `workflows/generate-ai.ts:1057` still declared `freeExhausted: true` as a LITERAL, so on the cheap GENERATION path a revoked free key or one unparseable free response billed summary/chapters/title on the paid API **with the free daily quota fully intact** — the owner's original complaint, alive in the other half of the pipeline. Two consequences of round 9's shared-helper deletion were also worse than disclosed: deleting the quota-wait exemption made generation's 4-hour paid flip **unreachable** (ERROR removes the row from the candidate set entirely), so during a free-quota outage the owner got "AI tahlil xato" instead of the analysis finishing on paid — the second half of his rule failing; and `autoRecoverAttempts` is never refunded on the generation path, so two parks pinned a video at the bound permanently and its retry button could not reset it.

**🎨 OWNER-FOUND IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE DEPLOY: the AI chat popup themes itself separately.** His words: *"umumiy dark bo'lsa dark, light bo'lsa light bo'lishi kerak, alohida alohida qilish ahmoqlik"*. Measured before answering him: `app/s/[videoId]/_components/ai-chat.css` is 757 lines with **42 hardcoded hex/rgb colours and ZERO app CSS variables**, plus its own `.ai-popup[data-theme="dark"]` palette; `AIChatPopup.tsx` carries a second theme mechanism (`readAppTheme`/`useAppTheme`, a `MutationObserver` on `document.body`, the `theme` cookie, `prefers-color-scheme`) that stamps `data-theme` on the popup. So it DOES follow the app — through a second mechanism painting a DIFFERENT palette, which is why its dark looks wrong next to everything else. Cause: the popup arrived as a delivered design drop-in (Wave 2.5) with its own stylesheet and was wired to the app theme but never converted to the app's tokens. **FIXED in `1357ad1` (3 files, +60/−95 — net negative, no CRLF churn).** Every colour is now a shared token (panel bg → `gray-1/2/4`, borders → `gray-6/7`, primary text → `gray-12`, secondary → `gray-11/9`, accent → `blue-9/11`, shadows via `color-mix` on the already-imported `--gray-a12` alpha scale), and the ENTIRE component-level mechanism is deleted: the `[data-theme="dark"]` palette block, `readAppTheme`, `useAppTheme`, the `MutationObserver`, the cookie read, the `data-theme` attribute and the `body.dark .ai-fab` override. Tokens flip with the app by themselves, so no second mechanism is needed. **Verified by the main session, not taken on the subagent's word: `tsc -b --force` 0 · web suite 1347 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0 · `grep` shows 0 colour literals left in `ai-chat.css` and 0 residual theme logic in `AIChatPopup.tsx`.** A new test fails on any colour literal reintroduced into that stylesheet (proven RED→GREEN), with an empty exception list. Generalised to the brain as `feedback_theme_belongs_to_tokens_not_components`. **Blind review returned SHIP on the structure** — one theme mechanism, correct scope (the theme class sits on `<html>` AND `<body>`, the popup is a body descendant with no portal escape, so it follows in both directions), hierarchy preserved, `--text-secondary` confirmed theme-invariant white, all 12 tokens resolve. **But it caught a real contrast REGRESSION, fixed in `eb9b521` before deploy:** `blue-9` is byte-identical in light and dark (`#0090ff`), so white-on-accent fell to **3.26:1** — below AA for the user's own chat messages, the most-read text in the panel (the old hardcoded `#2f6bef` was 4.70:1). Worse on the send button in dark: white on `blue-11` (`#70b8ff`) = **2.10:1**, a ghost arrow. Fix uses a self-flipping pair — fill `blue-11`, ink `gray-1` — because those two move in OPPOSITE directions with the theme, so contrast holds in both without a single `.dark` rule. **Measured, not eyeballed: 4.65 light / 8.98 dark on the five on-accent sites; 12.30 / 14.44 on the gradient's other stop.** The guard was also extended: the new test now parses every `var(--token)` in `ai-chat.css` and fails if it is undefined anywhere (proven RED with a real injected `var(--gray-13)`) — the repo's existing scale test only walks `.ts/.tsx` and only matches Tailwind class names, so nothing had covered this file. **Disclosed and deliberately NOT fixed (out of scope, improved not caused by this change): `.ai-citation` text on `--accent-soft` is 3.87:1 in light, below the 4.5 body floor — it was 2.70:1 before.** Also for the record: `gray-dark-alpha.css` is not imported, so `--gray-a12` does not flip — fine for a shadow, do not assume otherwise later.

It found the accent change had silently FIXED a second failure too: the chip hover border was 2.99:1 with `blue-9` and is 4.37:1 now.

**💡 MEASURED 2026-08-11 NIGHT — THE FREE TIER IS COUNTED IN REQUESTS, NOT TOKENS, AND THAT IS THE BIGGEST UNTAKEN COST LEVER.** Verbatim from Google in the prod log: `Quota exceeded for metric: generativelanguage.googleapis.com/generate_content_free_tier_requests, limit: 20, model: gemini-3-flash`. So it is **20 requests per day per model** — not hourly, not token-based. Two free models in the chain ≈ 40 free requests/day. Confirmed in the ledger: `gemini-3.5-flash` billed **$0.0000** for exactly 20 calls today, 23 yesterday, 22 the day before — the cap, hit every day.

Today's average request carried only **~5,150 input tokens** (1,350,254 over 262 calls) against a model context around 1M — requests are roughly two orders of magnitude smaller than they could be. At ~20-minute chunks the same recording would be ~4 requests, and several videos a day would fit entirely inside the free tier.

This needs a measured A/B on one real recording (same audio, 3-min vs 15-min vs 20-min chunks, compare cue timing drift and word accuracy) before any change. Owner raised the cost question; the work is NOT started.

**RUNNING TALLY: 10 build rounds, 10 blind reviews, 1 approach judgement, 38 defects — gates caught 0.** The rounds that finally shrank the surface are the ones that DELETED machinery instead of adding an owner for it. **Six separate times a test written by the implementer PINNED THE DEFECT instead of the rule** (three in round 9's sweep, three more found in round 10) — that, not missing coverage, is why ~1300 green tests proved nothing here.

**ROUND 8 (DELETION ROUND, committed after `3a8a029`) — the coordinator's own corrections: the invariant was his extrapolation, not the owner's rule, and the Batch machinery it forced is gone.** The owner's verbatim rule ("imkon qadar bepuldan foydalanadi, u tugasa qaytguncha pullikdan foydalanadi") means cheap must not pay WHILE FREE IS AVAILABLE — paying once free is out is the REQUESTED behaviour. Cheap transcription is now: per chunk, try the free chain; if it fails, run the SAME synchronous paid call immediately; the next chunk tries free again. DELETED: `submitCheapChunkBatch` and all cycle bookkeeping, the quota-park (`markQuotaWaiting`) and its transcription `AiQuotaWaitError` signalling, the recovery cron's quota-park ladder branch and `markQuotaLadderExhausted` — a cheap job now terminates exactly like a fast one (COMPLETE, or gap-per-bad-chunk, or ERROR + alert after bounded re-kicks; both-free-AND-paid-exhausted = ERROR/QUOTA, recovered by the pre-existing backoff loop when credits return). The gate enforces the CORRECTED invariant (cheap sync key only against an explicit `freeExhausted` declaration; every authorization carries mode+route, ops-script included). Fresh owner-initiated starts refund `autoRecoverAttempts`/`quotaRetry*` at the claim, so retry works after any terminal state. Cheap gap-fill is FREE-ONLY (chose skip-paid over cap). `poll-batch-jobs` kept UNUSED-GOING-FORWARD solely to drain legacy in-flight batches — nothing produces new ones. Locales rewritten (no "discounted queue" claims for new jobs; batch strings only behind the data-derived legacy `aiBatchPending` flag). Net diff: −327 lines.

**ROUND 9 (committed after `d3c3e30`; ship gate on round 8 said DO-NOT-SHIP with three small corrections).** (1) `freeExhausted` is now a classified FACT: only a genuine quota/rate-limit signal from the free chain (or no free key at all) sets it — a revoked key, safety block or malformed response RETHROWS as a real error into the existing retry→gap machinery, so a broken free key can no longer silently bill every cheap chunk on the paid path (the owner's original defect in the shape round 8 itself created by hardcoding `freeExhausted: true`); the owner's personal key now passes the SAME gate authorization (`authorizePaidGeminiUse`) instead of short-circuiting it, and the test that ASSERTED the bug ("free failure any reason → paid") was rewritten to assert the owner's rule. (2) The cheap+aiQuotaWaiting exemption in `shouldAutoRetry`/`withIncrementedAutoRecoverAttempts` is deleted — its bounding ladder went in round 8, so the retry budget now applies to every row (prod measured: zero parked rows today). Consequence, disclosed: cheap GENERATION quota-parks are now also bounded (≤2 re-kicks → ERROR + alert) and will usually die before their 4h paid flip — bounded and visible, but a behavior change in the generation phase that was otherwise out of scope. (3) Job-less operations (translation/chat/ops-script/owner-continue-paid) no longer HAVE a mode field to falsify — mode exists only on job-bound uses where it is derived from the job's own `aiSpeedMode`.

**ROUND 10 (committed after `91390d6`; the ship gate found the blast-radius miss both sides skipped — the GENERATION twin).** (1) `callAiApi` now computes `freeExhausted` as the same classified fact as transcription: only every-free-model-quota-failed (or no free key) counts; one non-quota failure (revoked key, permission error, unparseable free response) RETHROWS instead of silently billing summary/chapters/title on paid with free daily quota intact. (2) CHOICE MADE: generation got the same INLINE free→paid shape as transcription — genuine exhaustion pays immediately and carries on, so nothing parks; the park writer (`aiQuotaWaiting: true` in lib/generate-ai) and the LAST 4h flip (generation branch + `shouldAutoFlipCheapJobToPaid` + its constant) are DELETED. No automatic paid escalation exists in either phase now; the owner's manual continue-paid button is the only one. (3) Generation refunds `autoRecoverAttempts` on fresh owner starts and on success; cron re-kicks mark `isRetry: true`. (4) A transcription that COMPLETEs with failed-chunk gaps fires an ops alert — visible, not silent. Blast-radius sweep of every shared helper/flag touched in rounds 8-10 is in the session report (dead-but-harmless leftovers noted there: three `isAiQuotaWaitError` rethrow guards in generation fan-outs, the `aiQuotaWaiting` legacy readers in the panel).

## ▶ (previous) NEXT STEP (2026-08-10 morning)

**✅ OWNER-REPORTED UX DEFECT, ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED: pressing "AI tahlil" and reloading made the progress vanish**, leaving him unable to tell a running job from a cancelled one. Cause, found in code and confirmed against the DB: `transcribeVideo` launches the workflow WITHOUT claiming the row first — `transcriptionStatus` only becomes PROCESSING inside the workflow, after it has fetched the video, the owner and the org settings. A reload in that window reads NULL and the panel correctly renders "not started". `startAiGeneration` already claimed its job (QUEUED before launch); transcription was the one path that did not. Now claimed synchronously with `processingStartedAt`, so the existing stale-job cron can still clear a dead claim. 4 new tests pin the ORDERING (claim before launch) — they are RED against the old code.

**📊 GEMINI COST — measured, and my own first extrapolation was wrong; the corrected numbers are these.**
- The free tier is **20 requests per day PER MODEL** — that is not inferred, prod logs say `limit: 20` verbatim. Two free models ≈ 40 free calls/day.
- One 50-minute meeting costs ~33 transcription + ~23 summary calls, so **the free tier covers roughly one short recording a day**; everything past that falls back to paid, which is exactly what the logs show (`all free-tier models failed … falling back to paid`).
- The free key is NOT broken — probed live, both models answered 200. It was simply spent for the day.
- **Actual spend: last month $7.27; this month $1.45 in 10 days.** The org's budget cap is $11.63/month with an alert at 75%. I first extrapolated "~$12/month" from two unusually heavy days (two new long recordings landed on 9–10 Aug) — that was wrong and is corrected here so nobody plans against it.
- The lever if cost ever matters: "arzon" (cheap) mode waits for free quota instead of paying; it is per-job today and `organizations.settings.aiSpeedMode` is unset, so the default is fast/paid-first.

**🐞 THE THUMBNAIL WARNING IN THE LOGS LED TO A BIGGER, REAL DEFECT — now fixed.** The warning itself (`Can't load image …/screenshot/screen-capture.jpg: o is not iterable`, twice in 48h) is the OG-image renderer failing to decode a frame; it degrades to a gradient card and is NOT the dashboard thumbnail, so it is smaller than it first looked and is still open. **But chasing it exposed this:** `GET /api/video/preview?...&fallback=og` answered `302 Location: https://0.0.0.0:3000/api/video/og?...` on production. Every social scraper — Telegram, WhatsApp, Slack — followed that to nowhere, so **every shared recording link showed a blank preview card**, on a product whose entire distribution is people pasting links.
- Cause: the fallback was built with `new URL(path, request.url)`, and behind the Coolify/Traefik proxy `request.url` is the container's own origin (`HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0`, `PORT=3000`). **The public base-URL envs were all correct** — `WEB_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_WEB_URL` and `NEXTAUTH_URL` all read `https://data365.duckdns.org` — so reading an env would have hidden this rather than fixed it.
- Fix: no absolute URL at all. A relative `Location` is resolved by the client against the URL it actually requested, so it is right on any host — which matters specifically because this app is about to move to the client's Railway domain.
- 3 tests, RED against the old code. They strip comments before asserting, because the fix's own comment quotes the broken pattern.
- **✅ VERIFIED LIVE on prod image `c2e9551`:** the same request now answers `302 Location: /api/video/og?videoId=…` — relative — and following it end-to-end returns a real `image/png`, 1200×630, 43 KB. The blank-preview defect is closed.
- **❌ THE "THUMBNAIL DEFECT" WAS NOT A DEFECT. I was wrong twice, and the correction matters more than the original claim.** I reported "7 of 14 recordings have no thumbnail — half the library is broken" and blamed the browser extension for never saving one. Then I probed the actual files:
  - **6 of those 7 are flagged `isAudio: true`** — the owner and the team deliberately converted them with the replace-with-audio feature. `ffprobe` confirms the stored objects contain an audio stream and no video stream at all. A file with no picture cannot have a picture; that is the feature working, not a bug.
  - The 7th (`0vghhj9wm8smamw`) has **no playable source object at all** — a separate, real issue worth its own look, unrelated to thumbnails.
  - **Every recording that still has video also has its thumbnail.** So the extension path is NOT failing to produce one, and my grep-based conclusion ("the extension has no thumbnail code, therefore extension recordings have none") was an inference the data contradicts.
  - **I had already written the server-side poster-frame step, with tests, and reverted it**: shipping a fix for a defect that does not exist is pure added complexity. Nothing of it remains in the tree.
- **The lesson, since it cost real work:** "no thumbnail" is a symptom with at least three causes — no video track, no source file, and a genuine generation failure — and I jumped from the symptom straight to a cause I had grepped for, without asking the file itself what it contained. One `ffprobe` at the start would have ended it.

## ▶ (previous) NEXT STEP (2026-08-06 evening)

**PROD = `b38901c`, `/api/health` → `db:ok storage:ok`.** Live and verified: audio download (share page + card menu), bulk replace-with-audio, the card `⋮` fix, the four AI-chat fixes, the chat full-screen toggle, and the dead colour scales.

**`7bf7d63` (the audio-quality fix) was deployed once both encodes finished** — landing not yet confirmed at the time of writing, so check `docker ps` image tag against `git rev-parse` before believing it is live.

**A CONFLICT THAT IS REAL, unlike the ones I imagined:** a deploy replaces the app container, so it KILLS any job running inside it. That is what stopped the first big encode. If a container-resident job is running, name it before deploying. (The job itself is safe to lose — nothing is overwritten until the new file is probed and proven.)

**✅ REPLACE-WITH-AUDIO WAS EXERCISED FOR REAL, and it exposed a defect no test could have.** The owner ran it on `fm3pz7yref328m7` (a 53-minute client meeting). It worked — `isAudio: true`, the object is `audio/mpeg`, transcript and summary intact. **But the surviving audio is mono / 24 kHz / 64 kbit/s — telephone quality — and the video is gone.** Cause: the action called `extractAudioToBuffer`, which is deliberately machine-grade because its consumer is Gemini. This is the one place where the audio is EVERYTHING THAT SURVIVES. Fixed in `7bf7d63` (48 kHz / 128k / stereo) with a RED-first test that pins which extractor is used. **That one recording cannot be recovered — do not offer to.**

**✅ TWO UNCOMPRESSED RECORDINGS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, owner chose "compress, drop the original".** Measured over the live playlist route with a 1-byte ranged request against durations from the prod DB: of 9 recordings over 5 minutes, seven store at 0.5–1.3 MB/min and two were raw.
- `jbemr633th8ct7t` (11.8 min upload): **269.0 MB → 34.2 MB, 7.9:1** — done, verified, the app now serves the compressed `result.mp4` and the raw source is deleted. **235 MB freed.**
- `jm2ngbqg97wxk5t` ("Bunyod & Aman agency. deal 2", 53.7 min): **952.2 MB → 217.6 MB, 4.4:1 — done, verified live. 735 MB freed.** Its first encode DIED about 40% in, and the cause was in the recording, not the script: a decode-only pass prints "non monotonically increasing dts" hundreds of times — **this recording carries duplicate timestamps, the signature of the chunked recorder.** Fixed by regenerating the timeline (`-fflags +genpts`, `-fps_mode cfr`, `aresample=async=1`); the duration gate is what proves audio and video did not drift apart. **Worth knowing:** it still stores at ~4 MB/min against ~0.7 for the other meetings — probed and NOT a defect of the repair, the video track is an honest 406 kb/s at CRF 21 because this recording has more motion than a slides-only call.
- **Total freed tonight: 970 MB.**
- **Do not re-derive the method:** the app container has ffmpeg but NO aws-sdk and NO curl, so the script (`/root/recompress.mjs` on the VPS, copied in with `docker cp`) does SigV4 with node crypto and transfers with global `fetch`. **And the source is not always `result.mp4`** — a video that never got optimized has none and is served straight from `raw-upload.mp4`; asking for `result.mp4` there is a 404, which is how this was found. Read the key off the playlist redirect.
- Encoder args are copied EXACTLY from `lib/video-convert.ts` (h264 CRF 21, long edge capped at 1920, aac 128k, faststart) so these files do not become the odd ones out.

**A working-style correction the owner made twice in one session, now in memory:** I twice ended a turn saying I would wait for a build or a reviewer to finish. That was wrong — a conflict means TWO WRITERS ON ONE FILE, and a build only READS the tree. The disjoint surfaces I was blind to: the prod server, the DB, storage. The measurement above was found in two minutes once I looked there. (A deploy vs. a container-resident encode, on the other hand, IS a real conflict — hence the warning at the top.)

Fix by letting an explicit brevity request win over the detail rule; leave other answers detailed.
  2. **Tasks and decisions are re-derived instead of looked up.** Every video already carries `metadata.aiSummary.tasks` (title, assignee, priority, deadline), `nextSteps` and `topics`, computed once at transcription. The chat ignores all of it and asks the model to rebuild both from the 10 retrieved chunks — retrieved by similarity to a *generic* question, so the context is close to arbitrary — and pays for it on every click. Feed the stored lists into the prompt context as authoritative.

**📡 UPLOAD HEALTH — MEASURED 2026-08-05, three throttled arms (the owner asked twice; this replaces the earlier guesswork).** Harness: `e2e-audio-proof/run-proof-inpage.mjs` with a new optional `THROTTLE_KBPS` on the mock server (default unset = unchanged). Logs kept as `soak-throttle-{A,B,C}.log`.
| arm | link | result |
|---|---|---|
| A (control) | unthrottled | **PASS** 361s/360s, 17 parts, audio -14.7 dB, post-Stop wait ≈ instant |
| B | 4 Mbit/s | **PASS** 371s/360s, 14 parts, -14.8 dB, no error, no stall |
| C | 800 kbit/s | **FAIL** `upload-never-completed-to-mock` — only 8 parts up during the recording, still uploading when the harness gave up |
**What C actually proves, stated carefully:** no crash, no error, no corrupted audio — the upload is simply SLOWER THAN THE RECORDING, so the backlog grows and the user must leave the browser open after Stop. **It is NOT a regression from the bitrate raise:** arm A produced ~1.9 Mbit/s of real data even with the 8 Mbit/s ceiling (the bitrate is a CAP, not a floor, and static screen content never reaches it), so the old 3 Mbit/s build would have drowned on an 800 kbit/s link too. The raise does move the WORST case (video-heavy screens) from 3 → 8 Mbit/s.
**Requirement to state to the client:** sustained upload ≥ the recording bitrate — ~2 Mbit/s for slides/documents, up to 8 Mbit/s when the shared screen is playing video.
**Also verified, not assumed:** the popup DOES render an "Uploading…"/"Finishing up…" view with byte progress (`popup/popup.ts:682-776`), so a lagging upload is visible to the user, not silent. And `tabconstraints=OK` in all three runs is live proof the new unprocessed-tab-audio constraints reach the real capture stage, with a control arm proving the detector has teeth.
**OPEN DECISION for the owner:** leave as is · adapt the bitrate down automatically when the link cannot keep up · or lower the ceiling. Not started.

**THE THREE QUESTIONS, ANSWERED WITH NUMBERS (2026-08-03):**
- **Vertex AI — NOT worth it.** Per-token price is IDENTICAL to the Developer API for the models we use; the difference is that **Vertex has no recurring free tier**, and most of our re-runs are free today, so the ~$7.16/mo bill would likely become ~$20-35/mo, plus GCP/IAM setup. It does genuinely unlock `audioTimestamp` (the 1-3s tier the Developer API rejects). Owner decision, but the recommendation is no.
- **2-hour recording — UNPROVEN, and the top risk is named.** Longest ever proven is 48 min. Biggest risk: `inpage-recorder.ts` fires `ondataavailable` every 200 ms and does an awaited round-trip per chunk → **~36,000 messages over 2h**, each JSON-serializing raw bytes; never exercised past ~14,400. Also unproven: MV3 service-worker survival across 2h, and the cumulative ~1 MB tail-loss window per SW eviction (`upload.ts:52-60`). Multipart part count is fine (~560 of 10,000). **Free test is now runnable:** `PROOF_MINUTES=120 node run-proof-inpage.mjs` from `apps/browser-extension/e2e-audio-proof` after `pnpm build` — fake Meet page, synthetic tone, mock server, no AI spend; babysit 60 s then walk away.
- **Storage/compression — the real problem is NOT the encoder settings.** Compressed videos land at **0.7-1.24 MB/min** (H.264 CRF 28, 720p cap, 96k audio) but **3 of 7 measured videos never got compressed at all** and are served raw at **8.5-9.9 MB/min**, two of them ALSO duplicated under `source/original.mp4`. So a 2h meeting is ~120 MB compressed vs ~1.1 GB raw. At 6.6 GB free: ~55 compressed 2h meetings vs ~6 raw ones. **Fix the videos that skip compression before touching CRF** — CRF is the one knob that actually threatens shared-screen text legibility.

**🔁 SECOND CODEX AUDIT (owner ran [`docs/CODEX_PROMPT_timestamp.md`](CODEX_PROMPT_timestamp.md) himself, 2026-07-30) — verdict + what it changed:**
- Confirms our framing: *"6.2s is the current practical Developer-API baseline, NOT a proven Gemini-only floor"*; do not re-wire the probe warp, do not ship a nonlinear retime map; our linear per-chunk rescale is the right shape (it has REAL endpoints — speech onset + silence-derived speech end — while silences/cue-density cannot identify a trustworthy curve).
- **NEW, UNTRIED, worth it: TIMECODE-VIDEO input.** Render each chunk as a black MP4 carrying the original audio plus a burned-in running `HH:MM:SS` clock, and tell Gemini to read the clock. That is an **independent** clock instead of the model re-localizing its own text — the exact failure that killed step ④. Cost ≈ 3.1× input tokens at low resolution.
- Also proposed: pinned `gemini-3.6-flash` A/B. **⚠️ DEPRIORITIZED on cross-project evidence Codex lacks:** on gemini-mic (2026-07-29) 3.6-flash leaked its own reasoning INTO the transcript on 2 of 3 real-voice segments and lost to `gemini-3-flash-preview` at 1/3 the price — see [[playbook_gotchas_llm]]. Test it only after timecode, in an isolated harness.
- Its rig-upgrade ask (12 meetings × 30 human-marked onsets = 360 labels) is unrealistic for this owner. **Cheaper substitute in flight: SYNTHETIC ground truth** — cut 6 real 14s speech excerpts out of a real meeting and concatenate them with exact 12s silences, so every excerpt's true start is known by construction (0, 26, 52 … s). No labelling, no rig noise. Script: session scratchpad `timecode-ab.mjs` (runs inside the app container; needs `fontfile=/app/apps/web/public/fonts/NeueMontreal-Bold.otf` because the container ships no system fonts).
- **✅ RESULT (2026-07-30, run twice, ground truth from ffmpeg `silencedetect`, not assumed): TIMECODE VIDEO GIVES NOTHING — DISCARDED.**
  | arm | median err | max err | input tokens |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | audio only (production today) | **5.0 s** | 10.0 s | 3 957 |
  | black MP4 + burned-in clock | **5.0 s** | 10.0 s | 15 084 (**3.8×**) |
  Per-excerpt errors are near-identical (`6.5,10,2,0,5,1` vs `7.5,10,3,0,5,0`). Gemini does not use an on-screen clock to time its cues — it estimates from audio either way. Do not re-try; do not pay 3.8× for it.
- **🎯 THE BIG SIDE-RESULT — THE RIG IS VINDICATED AND THE FLOOR IS REAL.** This test had EXACT ground truth (we built the timeline; onsets measured, and the measured onsets landed on the slots: 0.51/26.01/52.02/78.02/104/130 s — so no hidden bias). It returned **median 5 s / max 10 s** — which matches what the token-overlap rig reported on the real 25-min meeting (**6.2 s / 11.0 s**). Two independent measurement methods agreeing is the strongest evidence available without human labels: **~5–6 s median really IS Gemini's own timestamp accuracy on the Developer API**, not an artifact of our instrument. Codex's "the rig cannot distinguish 6 s from 4 s" concern is now bounded — it cannot resolve 1 s differences, but it is not off by 2× either.
- **CONCLUSION FOR THE OWNER'S "don't stop until the max is reached" DIRECTIVE: reached.** Four candidates were built or probed and three were killed by measurement (probe warp — worse; audioTimestamp — not on this API; timecode video — no gain at 3.8× cost); the one that worked (3-min chunks) is live. What remains is not an algorithm but a different product: Vertex AI (parked owner decision) or a paid alignment vendor (explicitly out of scope).

**📱 iPHONE QUESTION — ANSWERED WITHOUT A DEVICE (2026-07-30, owner asked for research instead of a test). The old STATE claim "the stored files are VP9-in-MP4" was TOO BROAD — corrected here with a full-library sweep.**
- Method: listed every stored recording, read the first 1 MB of each `result.mp4` (ftyp brand + a `vp09` scan), then ffprobe'd samples over the real viewer path (`/api/playlist?videoType=mp4`).
- **Result across all 27 stored `result.mp4` objects: 16 are non-VP9, 11 are VP9-in-MP4.** Confirmed by codec, not brand alone: `5nkafdeqkbd82be` → **vp9 Profile 0 1280×720 + opus**; today's real meetings → **h264 High 1280×720 + aac** (`hcjeyee20t4fvvp`, `9btpw8ce39f88a4`), one older raw → h264 Baseline.
- **The VP9 ones are all OLD: 2026-07-18 → 2026-07-27, 7 videos in the DB** (several are 0–1 min test clips; the real content ones are `t91bnh582gnhgy8` 7 min and a couple of short meetings). **Everything the team recorded after that — including all of today's meetings — is H.264.** So the iPhone risk is a historical tail, not the current pipeline.
- Why it matters anyway: Safari/iOS plays H.264 and HEVC in MP4 reliably; VP9-in-MP4 is not supported by Apple's playback framework, and those files also carry **Opus** audio, which Safari does not accept in MP4 either — so those 11 would fail on an iPhone twice over.
- **✅ DONE (owner said convert, but REAL recordings only — "not the test videos", and only if free). Executed 2026-07-30:** of the 11 VP9 files only **2 are real** (5.1 min and 7.4 min); the other 9 are 0.1–1.7 min test clips and were deliberately left alone (one, `a89p0t4m9py12jj`, is a corrupted fragment ffprobe cannot even read — left untouched, it is a 0-min clip). Converted `5nkafdeqkbd82be` (15.1→8.5 MB) and `t91bnh582gnhgy8` (27.7→10.8 MB) to **h264 High 1280×720 + aac**, verified afterwards BOTH in storage and (where public) through the real viewer path. **Every original is preserved as `result.vp9-original.mp4` — nothing deleted, fully reversible.** Cost: our own server's CPU (flat monthly fee) + ~19 MB extra storage; **no new bill**. Script: session scratchpad `vp9-convert.mjs` (has a `--dry` mode and a MIN_MINUTES guard).
- **Note for the Railway migration:** the `result.vp9-original.mp4` backups are extra objects in the bucket — decide during the move whether to carry them over (they are only worth keeping until someone confirms the converted files look right).
- 16 of 43 video folders have NO `result.mp4` at all (optimize failed historically; those serve the raw upload) — that is the same population as the old E6b storage-flag investigation, and the raw one sampled was H.264 too.

**✅ REPLACE-WITH-AUDIO WAS DEAD FOR EVERY VIDEO — FIXED 2026-08-03, RED→GREEN PROVEN.** Owner hit the generic toast "Videoni audioga almashtirib bo'lmadi". Root cause found in the PROD container log (not guessed): `Unrecognized option 'pipe:1' … Error splitting the argument list`, exit code 8, video `j1q9mjg8kewr2en`. The ffmpeg arg array ended with `"-pipe:1"` — a leading dash turns the stdout OUTPUT TARGET into an unknown option. ffmpeg 8.0.1 rejects it. Three things worth keeping:
- **The bug was in TWO places, not one.** The diagnosing agent found `extractAudioToBuffer` (`apps/web/lib/audio-extract.ts`); its copy-pasted twin `convertWavToMp3` in the same file had the identical typo and was NOT reported. Both fixed. Classic [[feedback_blast_radius]] twin — a reference search for the caller would never have surfaced it.
- **The existing test asserted the BUG as correct**: `__tests__/unit/audio-extract.test.ts` had `expect(args).toContain("-pipe:1")`, so the suite stayed green while the feature was 100% broken. Assertion corrected + a negative assertion (`not.toContain("-pipe:1")`) added, and a new test now pins the twin. A test that encodes the defect is worse than no test.
- **It failed SAFE**: `replace-with-audio.ts` writes and verifies the new audio object BEFORE deleting the original, and the throw happened first — so **no video was ever destroyed by this bug**. Do not "simplify" that verify-before-delete gate; it is what made a total failure harmless.
- Evidence: restored the typo → 2 tests RED on the assertion (both sites) → restored the fix → **15/15 GREEN**.
- Still open (deliberately not bundled): `CapCard.tsx` funnels every failure reason except `unsupported_source` into one generic toast, so the real error is server-log-only. That is why this took a live log read to diagnose.

**🎨 LATENT UI DEFECT FOUND 2026-08-03 while reviewing an agent diff — DEAD GREEN CLASSES, not yet fixed.** `packages/ui/style/tailwind.config.js` builds Radix token scales for **gray, blue and red only** — there is **no `green` scale**. So every `bg-green-3` / `text-green-11` / `border-green-6` in the app resolves to nothing and renders UNSTYLED. Known sites: `caps/components/web-recorder-dialog/HowItWorksPanel.tsx`, `web-recorder-dialog.tsx`, `InProgressRecordingBar.tsx`, `import/loom/ImportLoomPage.tsx`. The pattern that actually WORKS in this repo is the default palette with explicit dark variants, e.g. `AccessManagement.tsx:743` (`bg-green-50 border-green-200 dark:bg-green-900/20 dark:border-green-800`) — the new login "account deleted" banner uses that. Fix later by either adding `green: getColorScale("green")` to the config (one line, fixes all sites at once) or converting the sites; adding the scale is the smaller change but must be eyeballed in BOTH themes before it counts. Caught because a token-looking class name is not proof the token exists.

He installed v0.5.5 (in **Opera**, not Chrome — a variable nothing had tested), started an in-page recording on a real Meet call, and opened **"Add people"**. Twice:
- seconds in → **"Upload failed — Recording was too short"**, SW logged `[upload] Recording too small (1200 durable bytes)` (= an mp4 header and nothing after it);
- a minute in → the recording **silently stopped** and saved (no error, no notification he saw).

**CAUSE (found in code, not guessed):** the in-page reload guard (`sw.ts`, the `tabs.onUpdated` listener) finalized the recording on `status:"loading"`. Chromium reports **same-document history navigations** that way too, and Meet pushes a history entry for its in-call dialogs (that is why the back button closes "Add people"). A dialog was therefore indistinguishable from F5 at that listener. The guard itself is not new — it predates v0.5.5; the corruption fix is unrelated and stands.

**FIX COMMITTED `abcff39` (v0.5.6), NOT DEPLOYED — deploy is owner-gated.** The signal is now CONFIRMED before acting: wait 1500 ms, then ask the tab whether its in-page recorder is still running (new `IS_INPAGE_RECORDING` probe, answered in `meet-detect.ts`). A real reload destroys that document so nothing answers and the guard still fires; a dialog leaves the recorder running and the recording is kept. State is re-read after the wait so a normal stop in between is not double-finalized.
**Measured (script-computed):** new test **RED without the sw.ts hunk → GREEN with it**; extension suite **146/146** (145 baseline + 1); root typecheck **exit 0**; sources have no whitespace churn (107 = 107 with `-w`); manifest bumped **0.5.5 → 0.5.6**, dist rebuilt, both zips repackaged and verified to CONTAIN the probe in `background.js` AND `content.js`. v0.5.6 also releases the previously-held **mic-missing warning**.

**REVIEW ROUND DONE — verdict SHIP-WITH-FIXES, both must-fixes CLOSED in `938d75d`.** The reviewer falsified the one-shot probe in BOTH directions: (1) a busy Meet main thread answers late, and reading that silence as "gone" would kill a live recording under exactly the load the bug happens under; (2) on a SLOW reload the old document is still alive at the first probe and answers "recording", so one answer was not proof of a dialog either — the recording would strand in "recording" forever. The probe is now POLLED (4 attempts, 2.5 s apart) and only a rejected sendMessage / a fresh `recording:false` counts as proof; an all-inconclusive run keeps the recording (stranding is recoverable by pressing Stop, a killed meeting is not). Both new tests **RED against `abcff39` → GREEN**; suite **148/148**; typecheck **0**; zips re-verified.

**⚠️ ONE CAUSE NOT YET RULED OUT (reviewer finding 3):** a SECOND path can end an in-page recording — `meet-detect.ts` `isInMeeting()` stops seeing a "Leave call" control for 5 s → `MEET_CALL_ENDED` → `STOP_CAPTURE` (age-gated at 10 s, so it cannot explain the seconds-in failure, but it fits the minute-in one). Deliberately NOT patched blind: that path is now NAMED in the service-worker log, so the next live test tells the two causes apart in one glance instead of costing another owner round-trip.

**✅ DEPLOYED + LIVE-VERIFIED (owner said "ha chiqar", 2026-07-28 ~21:43).** Coolify deployment `nyirnyce3c262hy63dt39lzo`. Prod app image = **`3029cd7`** (was `f43c7d3`), `/api/health` → `db:ok storage:ok`, the served zip is **0.5.6** and its `background.js` CONTAINS the fix (checked inside the artifact, not by its existence). Re-verified a second time at ~22:06.

**✅ OWNER RE-TESTED v0.5.6 ON A REAL MEET (2026-07-28 ~22:30) — THE DIALOG FIX HOLDS.** Screenshot evidence: the People / "Add people" panel OPEN while the recording ran on at **02:20**, no error card. That was the acceptance test for this stream, and it passed.

**🆕 HE THEN CAUGHT A SECOND, SEPARATE DEFECT — a FALSE alarm, fixed as v0.5.7 (`fe508ef` + test `e2f5dc1`).** Alone in a Meet, after two minutes he got *"The other person's audio isn't coming through"*. Nothing was wrong — nobody was speaking. His objection is the one that matters and is recorded verbatim in substance: **his IT team will read that message and conclude recordings do not capture voices, so they will distrust recordings that are fine.** On the product's core promise a false alarm costs more than no alarm. The watchdog fires on SILENCE and cannot tell "nobody is talking" from "the tap is broken", so it no longer asserts the second: it now states the observation and makes the action conditional. **The trigger is deliberately unchanged** — a genuinely lost tap must still surface. Suppressing it by counting participants was considered and REJECTED on evidence: Meet's People badge read **"2" while he was alone**, because his own screen share counts as a participant, so that heuristic would have traded one wrong answer for another. Checked for twins: the recorder-page warnings (muted / dropped / waived) are event-driven, not silence-driven. Measured: new test **RED against the old copy with the export in place** (fails on the assertion, not a missing import) **→ GREEN**; suite **149/149**; typecheck **0**; zips at **0.5.7**, new wording present and old wording gone inside `content.js`.

How to interrogate a deployment and the raise-the-heap fallback are now in [`docs/RUNBOOK.md`](RUNBOOK.md) §3a — worth keeping because a failed build is INVISIBLE from outside (green health, working site, change absent).
