## (history) RESUME 2026-07-23 — the blocker, now fixed

**The owner installed v0.5.4 and recorded — and the recording came back BROKEN. Root cause is found and proven; the fix is designed but NOT written.**
Full evidence + reproduction + the exact fix plan: **[`docs/HANDOFF_2026-07-23_broken-recording.md`](HANDOFF_2026-07-23_broken-recording.md)** — read it before touching anything.

One-paragraph version: the upload SUCCEEDS (22 MB really is in R2), but `result.mp4` is **two fMP4 streams interleaved** — fragment sequences `19…31` and `1…13` alternating, with the single `ftyp`+`moov` sitting 532 KB into the file instead of at the front. A browser cannot parse it (`MediaError.code 4`), so the share page shows "Could not load a playable video source" over a cap that otherwise looks normal (duration 1:23). The extension's other symptom — the modal **"Recording was too short — no video was captured"** — is the same failure wearing a misleading label (`upload.ts:1017` measures durable BYTES but the copy says duration).

**Root cause (confirmed in code):** `RECORDER_CHUNK` at [`sw.ts:1377`](../apps/browser-extension/src/background/sw.ts) carries **no recording/upload/session id** — `index` restarts at 0 per recorder — so the SW appends whatever arrives to whatever upload is live. A MediaRecorder still running from an EARLIER session (its media timeline is ~7.6 min in) wrote its fragments straight into the new recording. The `sw.ts:1248` guard only refuses a second `RECORDER_STARTED`; it does nothing about an already-running recorder's chunks. `RECORDER_STOPPED` has the same hole.

**Good news, proven not assumed:** the owner's actual recording is intact. Rebuilding `init + frags 1…13` from the same bytes yields a **playable 75.5 s / 1920×1080** file.

**▶ NEXT STEP:** write the regression test FIRST (feed the SW an interleaved two-id chunk stream, assert only the live id's bytes land — must go RED), then stamp all three senders + the SW handlers with the recording id. Blast radius and the exact line list are in the handoff §6.

### 🌙 2026-07-27 night — FIX IN PROGRESS (owner asleep, authorized "muammo bo'lmasin, fablega/codexga tekshirtir")
- **Handoff evidence re-verified independently before trusting it** (it was 4 days old): `sw.ts:1377` (CHUNK) and `:1390` (STOPPED) really do no identity check; all three senders really post only `{index, mime, ts}`; `sw.ts:1248` really blocks only a second `RECORDER_STARTED` *registration*. Confirmed, not assumed.
- **Handoff work COMMITTED (`e1ec42b`)** — it had sat UNTRACKED for 4 days ("git is the backup" was not holding for the single most load-bearing analysis in the project).
- **SECOND defect found while tracing, NOT in the handoff — must ship in the same change:** in all three senders the `finishStop`/`ondataavailable`/`onerror` closures read the **module-level mutable `state`** instead of their own session (`inpage-recorder.ts:560-585`, same shape in `offscreen/recorder.ts:248-275`, `recorder-page.ts:762-790`). A late `onstop` from an OLD recorder therefore runs `cleanup(state)`/`state = null` against the **CURRENT** recording. SW-side id filtering cannot fix this — it is client-side state. An id-stamp alone would leave the stale recorder running (mic/share indicator lit, chunks rejected forever).
- **PLAN written:** [`docs/plans/2026-07-27-recorder-session-id-fix.md`](plans/2026-07-27-recorder-session-id-fix.md) — session-id on all 4 message types + SW drops-and-logs mismatches, PLUS client-side ownership checks and explicit orphan teardown. Regression test FIRST (interleaved two-session chunk stream → only the live session's bytes land). Blast radius mapped by grep: **18 send sites** across 3 senders, 4 SW handlers, 5 test files. Open question in the plan: strict-drop vs accept an UNSTAMPED message while the live recording has an id.
- **Plan under INDEPENDENT review by BOTH Fable and Codex** (owner un-retired Codex for today: "codex bor faqat bugun undan ham foydalan" — two different model families = uncorrelated blind spots, which was the documented value of Codex). Build starts only after their verdicts are folded in.
- **✅ BUILT (v0.5.5), machine-proven RED→GREEN. NOT committed at the time of writing; NOT deployed — deploy stays owner-gated.** What landed in the working tree:
  - `shared/recorder-session.ts` (NEW) — one `newSessionId()` for all three recorder paths, deliberately single-sourced so three copy-pasted generators cannot diverge (the twin trap).
  - `background/upload.ts` — `isForeignRecorderSession()` + `handleChunk(..., expectedSessionId)` now returns `"accepted" | "stale"` and validates against **its own** state read (the TOCTOU fix both reviewers demanded).
  - `background/sw.ts` — `RECORDER_STARTED` persists `recorderSessionId`; CHUNK / STOPPED / ERROR validate **inside `enqueueRecorder`** and answer `{ok:false, reason:"stale-session"}` so the loser can retire itself; `finalizeRecording(expectedSessionId?)`.
  - `background/state.ts` — new persisted `recorderSessionId` field.
  - All three senders (`inpage-recorder.ts`, `offscreen/recorder.ts`, `recorder-page.ts`) — mint the id BEFORE any await, stamp every RECORDER_* message, capture their own session (`isMine()`) so a late callback can no longer tear down the CURRENT recording, and `retireOrphan()` stops the recorder + releases tracks/AudioContext when the SW says the session is stale (otherwise an orphan keeps the mic/share indicator lit forever).
  - `sw.test.ts` — new **F2** block, 5 tests: stale chunk dropped · live chunk still accepted · UNSTAMPED chunk dropped (strict) · stale STOPPED does not finalize · stale ERROR does not tear down.
- **MEASURED (script-computed, not self-assessed):** F2 **RED before the fix (4 failing) → GREEN after**; full extension suite **141/141** (136 baseline + 5 new); root `pnpm typecheck` **exit 0**; **no CRLF churn** (409 = 409 with `-w`); extension bumped **0.5.4 → 0.5.5**, dist rebuilt, both zips repackaged and verified to CONTAIN the fix (`stale-session` present in the packaged `background.js`, `sessionId` in `content.js`/`offscreen.js`) — written AND wired, not just written.
- **✅ 2026-07-28: ALL 13 REVIEW DEFECTS CLOSED (commit `4ab247c`, pushed).** Owner directive that morning: *"barcha muammoni hal qil va keyin butun loyihani qayta audit qilib production ready qil."* Rounds 2+3 fixed every defect from both reviewers as ONE coherent change (they are all "two sessions coexist"): atomic + id-required `RECORDER_STARTED`; a bounded `retiredSessions` list checked at the edge AND inside the queued job (the only way to judge a stray message while a NEW attempt is *arming*, when no live id exists); a refused start answering `reason:"start-in-flight"` so the loser bows out QUIETLY instead of killing the winner with an error; arming-phase errors accepted only from the context that armed it; `restorePendingBufferIfNeeded` re-verifying the upload is still live before pasting stored bytes (dropping the chunk afterwards does NOT un-mix bytes already pasted); identity re-checked immediately before `appendToBuffer`; and per-sender `isMine()` / `retireOrphan()` / `releaseOwnMedia()` tracked from acquisition. **Measured: vitest 145/145, typecheck 0, zips repackaged at v0.5.5 and verified to CONTAIN this round's markers.** A FINAL review round (both reviewers, on the whole diff) is the last gate before asking the owner to deploy. Next after that: the whole-project re-audit, planned in [`docs/plans/2026-07-28-production-ready-audit.md`](plans/2026-07-28-production-ready-audit.md) — whose key new rule is that every stateful surface must be exercised TWICE, because the 07-22 61-test QA could never have caught this bug: it only ever tested ONE recording.
- **(history) CODEX DIFF-REVIEW VERDICT = BLOCK — the round-1 fix was INCOMPLETE. DID NOT DEPLOY v0.5.5 AS IT STOOD (committed `bad5338`, pushed, NOT deployed — prod is still `16a93d8`/v0.5.4, untouched).** Fable reviewed the same diff and said SHIP-WITH-FIXES; its 4 findings were fixed before the commit (incl. falsifying my unstamped-message leniency). Codex, reading the POST-fix code, agreed the asymmetry is now closed but found **9 further defects, 4 CRITICAL** — all in the same race family the change is about, so they cannot be waved off. Recorded verbatim-in-substance so morning-me does not re-derive them:
  1. **CRITICAL — `sw.ts` RECORDER_STARTED is not atomic.** Two starts read the same `arming` state and BOTH create an upload; the last `setState` wins and the other upload + recorder is orphaned. (Codex flagged this at PLAN stage too and I did not fix it — that is on me.)
  2. **CRITICAL — an UNSTAMPED `RECORDER_STARTED` creates a live state with no id**, after which `isForeignRecorderSession` returns false for everything and the ORIGINAL interleaving hole is fully reopened. The guard is only as strong as the weakest start path.
  3. **HIGH — no identity check while `arming`.** A stale `RECORDER_ERROR(A)` arriving while B is arming still flips state to error and closes B's offscreen document.
  4. **CRITICAL — `upload.ts` chunk passes the identity check, then awaits `restorePendingBufferIfNeeded`; CANCEL + START(B) swap the buffer; A resumes and `appendToBuffer` mutates B's buffer.** The later fresh-state guard returns "stale" but A's BYTES ARE ALREADY IN B's buffer. My D4 fix closed the state write, not the buffer write.
  5. **HIGH — track `onended` callbacks (all three senders) still read the global `state`,** so A's dying display track stops B's recorder, bypassing the session check.
  6. **CRITICAL — I INTRODUCED THIS ONE TONIGHT: `inpage-recorder.ts` catch uses `const failed = pending ?? state`.** If A's `pending` was already nulled, `failed` IS B — so A's failure cleans up B and reports the error under B's session id, which the SW then accepts as genuine.
  7/8. **HIGH — orphaned start attempts leak** their display/mic/tab-audio tracks and AudioContext (recorder-page + offscreen): the "don't clean up the winner" guard correctly skips B, but nothing then releases A.
  9. **HIGH — two unstamped `RECORDER_ERROR` send sites remain** (`inpage-recorder.ts`, `offscreen/recorder.ts`); harmless against a live recording, harmful during `arming` via defect 3.
- **▶ RESUME HERE ON THIS STREAM:** fix 1–9 as ONE coherent change (they are all "two sessions coexist" — piecemeal midnight patches would have shipped a new bug), then re-run F2 + full suite + typecheck, then a FRESH non-authoring review of the new diff, and only then ask the owner to deploy. Deliberately NOT attempted at 23:15 with a long context against a BLOCK verdict: nothing is at risk while it sits undeployed, and rushing a concurrency fix is how the next corruption ships.
- **KNOWN, DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY (put to both reviewers; Codex confirms it is now CLOSED for live recordings, the residual is defect 3 during `arming`):** an unstamped CHUNK is strict-dropped when the live recording has an id, but STOPPED/ERROR are only checked when the message CARRIES an id — because internal callers (`tabs.onRemoved`, crash resume) legitimately pass none. One send site stays unstamped by design: `offscreen/recorder.ts:455`, the `START_CAPTURE` catch outside `startCapture`, where no session exists yet.
- Deliberately NOT bundled: the misleading `upload.ts:1017` "Recording was too short" copy (it measures durable BYTES) — real user-facing lie, separate lower-risk change.

## 🎯 GOAL AFTER THIS ONE (owner, 2026-07-28): once production-ready, MIGRATE the project to the CLIENT's own **Railway** server.
Not started, deliberately — it begins only after production-ready. Recorded now so it is not a surprise, with the five things that decide whether it is easy or painful:
1. **Does the domain change?** The extension has `data365.duckdns.org` baked in. A new domain = rebuild the extension AND every team member reinstalls. Ask the owner before the move, not after.
2. **Cost shape flips.** Contabo is a flat monthly fee; Railway bills by usage, and this app does ffmpeg + long transcription work. Estimate before committing — this could be cheaper OR several times dearer.
3. **DB + VIDEO MIGRATION IS BACK ON — owner REVERSED the fresh-start decision on 2026-07-29 ("fikrim o'zgardi, endi saytdagi videolarni ham qo'shib ko'chiramiz").** The 2026-07-28 "noldan new start, eski datalar kerak emas" line is SUPERSEDED — do not plan against it. This restores the riskiest step of the move and changes what the migration is: the existing recordings (~25) and the DB rows that point at them must arrive on the new host still playable. Consequences to work through BEFORE the move, none actioned: a DB dump+restore (the videos table, users, orgs — a partial restore leaves orphan rows pointing at files nobody can reach); the R2 objects either stay in the CURRENT bucket (then the client's app depends on the old account's credentials — the thing a handover is supposed to end) or get COPIED into the client's own bucket (then every stored key/URL must be re-pointed, and the copy verified object-by-object before anything old is deleted); and nothing old gets deleted until the new host serves the same videos. **Still open from the earlier plan, unchanged:**
   - **The client needs their OWN Cloudflare R2 credentials/bucket.** Reusing the current ones would have a "fresh start" writing into the existing bucket, mixing two clients' recordings — the opposite of what a fresh start means.
   - **What happens to the OLD deployment** (data365 + its 25 videos)? Kept, or shut down and erased? Destructive and irreversible, so it waits for an explicit instruction; nothing will be deleted on assumption.
   - Fresh start also means a new admin account + organization created once on the new host (~5 min, `INITIAL_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `INITIAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD`).
4. ~~**The recordings do NOT move**~~ — **SUPERSEDED by the 2026-07-29 reversal above.** They live in Cloudflare R2 and are host-independent, so the HOSTING move still does not touch them — but "bring the videos along" now means deciding whose bucket holds them afterwards, and that decision is the owner's.
5. **RUNBOOK must be rewritten** — today's deploy/rollback procedure is Coolify-specific, and the handover promise is that a fresh person can deploy and roll back.

Baribir boshlaymizmi? (faqat ekran va mikrofon yoziladi)".** So a subsequent recording fails to re-capture the remote participants' voices.

**⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-07-20 evening — the paragraph below was half-wrong; read the correction FIRST.** The free tier is neither dead nor a workaround: it is **real but capped at ~20 calls/day**. Proof from the prod DB, not from logs:
```
SELECT operation, model, COUNT(*), SUM(costUsdMicros=0) FROM ai_usage_events WHERE videoId='fm3pz7yref328m7' GROUP BY 1,2;
  transcription  gemini-3.5-flash  20 rows  ALL $0     <- free audio transcription DID work, 20×
  summary        gemini-3.5-flash   8 rows  ALL $0
```
Daily totals show the same shape (07-19: 17 free calls, 07-11: 24). Directly measured the same evening: the **Files API upload on the free key succeeds** (1.4 MB / 6-min chunk → uploaded + ACTIVE in **3.3s**), so "the upload bucket is exhausted" was a symptom of that day's quota, not a permanent property. Audio `generateContent` on free then returned **503 UNAVAILABLE** 4/4 (one hanging 74s) — capacity, not quota.
**So the real defect is NOT "free never works".** It is: once the daily ~20 is spent, the code still burns the full 30 s probe on **every remaining chunk** before falling back to paid (7/7 chunks on the 07-20 run). The fix is one surgical change — remember the quota-exhausted signal and stop probing for the rest of the run — **not** deleting the free path, which would destroy a saving that is genuinely happening. Also measured: `gemini-3.5-flash` free quota is `quotaValue: 20` per model per day (from the 429 body); `gemini-3-flash-preview` still answered on the free key while 3.5 was exhausted, so switching the free model id may buy more headroom — **unverified, needs the owner's rate-limit page.**

**Also corrected 2026-07-20 (owner-measured): prod DOES auto-migrate on boot.** Logs show `DB migrations triggered → Running DB migrations`, and an already-present column logs `Migration error is benign (ER_DUP_FIELDNAME)`. The older lines at ~1587/1606 saying *"deploy auto-migrate QILMAYDI — qo'lda CREATE TABLE qildim"* are left in place as history, but they are **no longer true of today's deploy** — and they already cost real work: an adversarial plan review ranked a non-existent "total AI outage from a missing column" as its #1 blocker on the strength of them. Verify against a live boot log before trusting either claim.

**(superseded, kept for the trail) "No free-tier workaround exists — this was TESTED, so don't re-try it."** The free key is not uniformly dead: a direct `generateContent` call returns **HTTP 200**, which makes it look like a usable fallback. It is not, because transcription needs the **Files API** to upload audio first, and that bucket IS exhausted: forcing a run with `{"speedMode":"cheap"}` (the patient free path) produced `cheap-mode Batch submit failed … Gemini upload init failed (HTTP 429)`. The video parked correctly in `PROCESSING` + `aiQuotaWaiting` and will resume by itself when quota returns — that part of the design works. Text generation on free ≠ audio transcription on free.

**✅ THE FAILURE PATH IS NOW FIXED — committed `bbf3ae2`, unit-proven, NOT live-verified.** A parallel session finished it and ended without committing; the work was reviewed (gate exit 0, its 19 tests pass) and committed here so a stray checkout could not lose it. What it does: `transcriptionError.code` distinguishes **QUOTA** (out of credit / rate-limited — transient) from **FAILED** (a real failure); QUOTA is re-kicked by `recover-stale-ai-jobs` with a **backoff** (`quotaRetryNextAt`) rather than a flat attempt cap — an adversarial review caught that a flat 5-attempt cap burns out ~2.5 hours after the failure while credits are topped up the NEXT DAY, so nothing would ever have self-recovered; and the share page now says something true and audience-specific in three languages (owner: "your recording is safe, the AI account needs topping up"; public viewer: the calm version). ⚠️ **Neither branch has run against the real API** — every Gemini call is 429 — so re-verify the auto-retry once billing is restored.

**(historic) The same fix was designed earlier in the day and deliberately NOT shipped** (then spawned as its own task). Today the pipeline throws a generic `All N transcription chunks failed`, the video lands in `ERROR`, nothing resumes it when billing returns, and recovery meant flipping `transcriptionStatus` by hand in MySQL. It should instead name the cause, keep the recording obviously safe, and offer a retry. The reason it was not shipped mid-incident: **it is a change to the failure path, and with every Gemini call returning 429 there was no way to verify either branch.** Shipping unverifiable changes to the recovery path of the core feature, during an outage of that feature, is how a bad day becomes a worse one. Note for whoever picks it up: reusing `AiQuotaWaitError` in fast mode is a trap — cheap mode can park safely only because it has already submitted a durable Batch that a cron collects; fast mode would strand the video in PROCESSING forever.

How it was found: the run that would have verified the drift anchor fix failed with `All 9 transcription chunks failed … HTTP 429`. The honest part: **four re-transcriptions of a 53-minute video in one afternoon (mine, verifying the drift fix) contributed to draining it** — this month's AI spend moved 59 760 → 68 400 so'm during the session. A verification loop that re-runs a paid pipeline needs a budget check before the first run, not after the fourth. See also the free-tier finding below: with the free tier broken on both paths, every one of those runs billed the paid account.

## ❓ OPEN DECISION — TRANSCRIPT TIMING: pay for Scribe, or live with ~10s? (asked 2026-07-20, NOT yet answered)

The owner asked two things at the end of the session; here is what was established, so the question can be re-asked in one line instead of re-researched.

**Where free (Gemini-only) timing stands today, measured:** 52-minute meeting → **mean ~10s, max 28.6s**, systematic bias gone (−4.3s). For scale, the 2026-07-08 investigation on a 2-hour video reached **~11s mean** by a completely different free method (pause-reinsertion retime) and concluded transcript-only methods bottom out there, because Gemini's output carries almost no timing structure. ⚠️ The two numbers come from **different videos and different measuring rigs** — treat it as "same order of magnitude", not a like-for-like comparison.

**The paid alternative already EXISTS in the product and is wired end-to-end:** the "Timestampni aniqlashtirish" button (`apps/web/actions/video/refine-timestamps.ts`, `lib/eleven-scribe.ts`, strings at `messages/uz.json:183-190`). It keeps Gemini's TEXT and replaces only the TIMINGS via ElevenLabs Scribe v2 — measured at **~3s** on the 2h video in July. Per-video and opt-in, so it costs only for recordings whose timing actually matters: **~$0.22 per audio hour** (a 52-min meeting ≈ $0.19).

**But it cannot be used right now: `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` is NOT set on the prod container** (verified by `printenv` — empty). The button therefore answers "Scribe hali serverga ulanmagan". **To enable: owner supplies an ElevenLabs API key → set it in Coolify → redeploy.**

**✅ THE REVIEW CAME BACK — full text saved at `docs/plans/2026-07-20-free-timing-ceiling-review.md`. Read it before touching timing again; it is ~90k tokens of work and it changes the plan.** Verdict: **not the free ceiling, but the free headroom is ~2×, not ~10×.** Its three load-bearing points:
1. **Do 3-minute chunks first** (`LONG_AUDIO_CHUNK_DURATION_SEC` 360→180 in `workflows/transcribe.ts:113`). One linear stretch cannot remove a *curved* clock, only its average slope; halving the span caps the leftover curvature. Plausibly **mean 10→5-7s, max 28→12-15s — i.e. the ≤15s target, for ~$0.40 and 1-2 hours.**
2. **⚠️ The measuring rig is now a limiting factor.** Cue-start quantization alone is ±2-5s, weak matches (score 0.33, threshold 0.25) can be off by ~10s, and the standard error of a 12-sample mean is ~2.3s. **So "10s vs 7s" is not a distinguishable difference today, and the max=28.6s rests on the least trustworthy sample in the run.** Calibrate first (~30 min): run the rig against the bbzt Scribe VTT whose true error is ~1-2s — whatever it reports there IS the instrument's noise floor.
3. **A free path to ~3-5s exists and was never built:** grid-probe piecewise warp (turn the measuring rig into a correcting one, ~$0.05-0.10/hour, ~1 day, reuses the July `grid-localize.mjs`/`transfer.mjs`). Scribe stays the proven 1-2s answer at $0.22/hour.

**The decision for the owner:** (a) accept ~10s free, (b) supply an ElevenLabs key and refine per-video at ~$0.22/hour, or (c) spend ~2 more hours on 3-minute chunks first — which may reach the target for pennies and does not close off (b).

**Honest caveat to carry into that decision:** for the fast multi-speaker CARD ui, the 07-08 analysis showed even **3s** of drift lands on the wrong 4s card **38%** of the time. If the requirement is "the highlighted card is always right", no free transcript-side method gets there — only the grid-probe warp or Scribe.

**✅ THE JULY RETIME PROTOTYPE IS RECOVERED — `docs/plans/recovered-retime-prototype/` (34 files, commit `d0c922b`).** It was believed lost: the scratchpad that held it was emptied on 2026-07-15 and nothing had been committed. It came back out of the SESSION TRANSCRIPT — Claude Code keeps every session as JSONL under `C:\Users\mirko\.claude\projects\D--vibecoding\<session-id>.jsonl`, and a `Write` tool call contains the whole file. Method, reusable for any "lost" scratchpad: grep the transcript for a distinctive string, then walk the JSON for `tool_use` blocks whose `input.file_path` matches and dump `input.content` (keep the longest version = the final edit).
What is in it: `warp4.mjs` (the pause-reinsertion algorithm), `warp.mjs`/`warp2`/`warp3` (the three approaches that FAILED first — silence-envelope, onset-impulse, Viterbi — so nobody re-tries them), `retime-spec.md` (the production-port spec, algorithm declared frozen), `grid-localize.mjs` (the ground-truth rig behind the 18.3s→10.8s number), plus the diagnostics that proved Gemini compresses SPEECH, not only pauses. ⚠️ They hard-code the old scratchpad path and that one video's constants — take the ALGORITHM, not the scripts.
Two facts the hunt also settled: part of the July work WAS already tracked at `apps/web/scripts/scribe-poc/` (grid-localize, transfer-dp + data), and `apps/web/lib/vtt-retime.ts` **does not exist anywhere on the machine** — the production port was specced but never done.

**THE INSIGHT WORTH ACTING ON:** today's shipped rescale is **linear** (one factor per chunk — fixes the average slope); the July retime is **non-uniform** (silences absorb the missing time, speech keeps slope 1 — fixes the shape). Each reached ~10s ALONE. **Whether they compose has never been measured** — the July team had no rescale, and today's work did not have this. Measure in this order once credits return: ① current ② + 3-minute chunks (`docs/plans/2026-07-20-shorter-chunks-ready-to-apply.md`) ③ + retime port.

**State of the tree (2026-07-20 ~17:00):** everything is committed and pushed to `qa-fixes`, HEAD **`bbf3ae2`** (38 commits this session, including a parallel session's quota work that was rescued from an uncommitted state). `git status` shows **100 untracked files and 1 modified** — none of them are this session's work: they are long-standing leftovers (old `CODEX_TO_CLAUDE_*.md` handoffs, `docs/qa/**` reports, scratchpad dirs) that predate it. Do not "tidy" them blindly; each is someone's old evidence.

With that, the proof gets three stages further and confirms: keystroke → `chrome.commands` → the SW opens the recorder tab with the right params → the Boshlash click reaches `startRecording` (`btn-start disabled after click: true`, a check added to separate "click never landed" from "something after it hangs"). **What is still unproven is only the last stage: the screen picker is not auto-accepted in the recorder-page context, so `getDisplayMedia` never resolves.** Note the in-page proof auto-accepts fine with the same flag, so the difference is that context, not the flag.

An attempt to drive it without a keystroke also failed for a harness reason worth knowing: **`chrome.runtime.sendMessage` from inside the service worker does NOT reach that worker's own `onMessage` listener**, so a START_INSTRUCTION cannot be simulated from there. The owner's QA script names this path as the fallback, so his run covers it.
- Two real bugs it caught, both fixed: the content script's copy-pasted meeting-id regex kept a LEADING SLASH (so `MEET_CALL_ENDED` never matched a shortcut-path recording → the meeting-ended prompt silently never fired); and `getUserMedia` hanging on Chrome's mic prompt froze the start on "Boshlanmoqda…" (mic is now best-effort, 8s deadline).

**✅ INDEPENDENT REVIEW ROUND DONE (Fable, non-author) — 7 defects found, all fixed in `4cc00ca`.** Three were BLOCKERs and none were hypothetical:
1. **Meet tab RELOAD** mid-recording left the SW stuck in "recording" forever (beforeunload's `recorder.stop()` never runs its handlers in a dying document; `tabs.onRemoved` does not fire on reload; the URL watcher only reacts to URL *changes*). Everything after the reload went unrecorded while the badge said REC and a restart was refused. → SW now finalizes on `status==="loading"` for the recording's own content tab.
2. **Doubled/echoing audio**: start alone → picker system audio is mixed as a stand-in; the client joins → their voice is tapped AND still coming through the loopback. → system audio now has its own gain node, muted the instant a real tap attaches.
3. **The subtle one — Meet REUSES its media elements and swaps the source.** A `MediaStreamAudioSourceNode` is bound to the track it was built from, so taps went silent while still looking connected. My first fix (prune streams with no live track) was WRONG and the harness proved it: segments 3–4 at **−90 dB, digital silence**. Real fix = compare the bound TRACK ID to the element's current live track and rebuild on drift. Re-measured: all 4 segments at a steady **−16.6 dB**. Run it with `PROOF_SWAP=1 node run-proof-inpage.mjs`.
Also fixed: stale `arming` after declining the confirm (new sender-scoped `MEET_NUDGE_CANCEL`), watchdog warnings written into a DOM-detached card (invisible), a leaked mic stream after the 8s timeout, "Stop sharing" missed during the start window, `pickMimeType` hoisted out of THREE copies, idempotent PAUSE/RESUME acks.
**Known debt, deliberately unpaid:** the chunk-streaming machinery is still duplicated across the three recorder paths (recorder page / offscreen / in-page). Next time one of them changes, hoist it.

**✅ DRIFT FIX IS NOW PROVEN ON PROD — but the ≤15s target is NOT met (2026-07-20, owner approved the re-transcription).** Same video, same rig, same 6-sample method, before vs after re-transcribing `fm3pz7yref328m7` (53 min) with the deployed fix:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| max abs drift | **195.8s** | **25.0s** |
| mean abs | 51.2s | 10.5s |
| mean signed | −49.5s | −2.7s |

The systematic backward drift is GONE (−49.5s → −2.7s): that was the linear within-chunk clock error the fix targets, and the server logs show it firing per chunk (`cue-rescale x1.259 (speech span 359s)`). What remains is scattered, not systematic — one sample at −25.0s keeps max above the 15s target. **Do not claim "≤15s met".** Whether to chase the last 10s is the owner's call; the reading is now honest either way. Backup of the pre-run transcript: `/root/backup-fm3pz7yref328m7-20260720-1235.vtt` (restore path tested — see below).

**Two cost/robustness findings from the same run, worth acting on:**
1. **The free Gemini tier is never used — for TWO different reasons, so one fix will not cover both.** On the TRANSCRIBE side every chunk logged `Free probe aborted after 30000ms deadline` and fell back to paid (7/7); the paid call for the same chunk took ~360s, so a 30s probe may never finish by construction. On the AI-GENERATION side the same free key returns `HTTP 429: You exceeded your current quota` — the free key is exhausted or has no billing project behind it. So "bepul kvota bilan tejaldi" currently saves nothing on either path. **Fix them in this ORDER:** the 30s value is already flagged in the code as an untuned placeholder (`FREE_TRANSCRIBE_PROBE_MS`, "measured-p75 tuning is a blocked Phase-A follow-up"), but raising it BEFORE the key's quota is sorted would only make every chunk wait longer for the same fallback — i.e. slower AND still paid. Establish that the free key can answer at all first (owner-side: quota/billing), then tune the deadline against a measured p75.
2. **Gemini's dot-separated timestamps — FIXED in `9730cf5`, and they were also eating the drift fix.** A 12-sample re-measure still showed two bad samples (−18.7s, −66.7s) among ten good ones, which reads like an accuracy limit. It was a COVERAGE hole: the server logged 7 `cue-rescale` lines for 9 chunks, and both bad samples sat in a chunk that was never rescaled. Cause: Gemini writes cues as `00:00.000 --> 00:00.07.500` (= 0s, 7.5s, 14.5s read as a sequence), the pattern matched only the `00:00.07` prefix, every cue in that chunk collapsed to ~0.07s → the chunk looked "under-covered" → a PAID retry, twice → too few parsable cues → rescale skipped → those minutes kept full drift. `parseTimestampToSeconds` now reads that shape, and the pipeline **logs the SKIP** too (its silence is what turned a glance into an investigation). Regression tests proven to bite. **MEASURED after the `9730cf5` deploy (re-transcribed + 12-sample rig):** the parser fix delivered exactly what it claimed and nothing more — **paid retries 2 → 0**, chunk@2890 now rescaled (x1.071), 9/9 chunks accounted for in the logs. But **max drift did NOT improve: 65.9s** (mean_abs 14.4s, mean signed −11.8s). So the ≤15s target is still **NOT met**, and the reason is now precisely known rather than guessed.

**ROOT CAUSE OF THE ONE REMAINING OUTLIER (do not re-derive this).** The new SKIP logging named it on the first run: `chunk@361.27s cue-rescale SKIPPED reason=factor-out-of-range factor=1.007`. That chunk's surviving cues stop at **311s of a 362s span** — it is badly compressed and should have been stretched by ~1.16 — but at rescale time a lone cue reached ~359.5s, so `max(end)` said "already the right length" and the rescale declined to run. Drift inside that chunk grows −18.5s → −65.9s, the worst error in the recording. **The fix is not wrong; it never fires there.** The anchor is a single point (`max(end)`), and one stray trailing cue defeats it.

**✅ RESOLVED IN `13dfa48` — and the numbers moved.** Once every chunk got rescaled (the run of 13:45), the sample that had sat at **−65.9s came back at −3.5s** and max fell **65.9s → 28.6s** (mean_abs 10.2s, mean signed −4.3s over 12 samples; a second 9-sample pass at different offsets agreed: max 28.6s, mean 10.2s). The remaining −28.6s is the same defect in a milder form — the anchor inflated rather than skipped — and `13dfa48` fixes it by anchoring on the chunk's BODY: a final cue is treated as a stray when its gap exceeds **3× that chunk's own median cue spacing**. That ratio is not a guess; the probe gave nine real chunks with final gaps of 2.5/4.0/5.0/5.0/5.0/5.0/5.5/6.0s against ~6s spacing, versus **16.5s** on the one chunk that misled it. ⏳ **Its end-to-end number is UNMEASURED and cannot be taken today: the Gemini quota is EXHAUSTED.** The verification run after the `13dfa48` deploy failed with `All 9 transcription chunks failed … Gemini upload init failed (HTTP 429)` — four re-transcriptions of a 53-minute video in one afternoon spent the day's budget. **Nothing was lost:** the good transcript from the 13:45 run survived untouched (461 cues, 75171 bytes, last cue 00:52:47 — verified by reading the object back), the failed run only wrote an 8-byte `transcription-partial.vtt` which has been deleted, and `transcriptionStatus` was flipped back from ERROR to COMPLETE so the video reads correctly. **To finish this: re-run ONE transcription of `fm3pz7yref328m7` on a fresh quota day, then the 12-sample rig. Everything else is already in place.** Current honest standing: max 28.6s (target ≤15s), with the fix for that exact 28.6s case deployed but unproven end-to-end.

**⚠️ The FIRST attempt at this was written and REVERTED, deliberately — do not re-try that shape.** Ignoring a final cue that sits alone after a >20s gap fixed the prod case but turned **5 existing rescale tests RED** — their fixtures are legitimately sparse, so "isolated last cue" also describes normal speech. One production observation is not enough to justify a heuristic that reinterprets every chunk. The honest next step is a better anchor (e.g. fit the cue distribution rather than take its maximum), designed against several real chunks, not one. **The data collection for that is already shipped (`76e2545`): every chunk now logs `anchor-probe cues=… last=… 2nd=… p90=… span=… gapBeforeLast=…`, so the owner's NEXT real meeting produces the evidence at no extra cost.** Read those lines with `docker logs <app> | grep anchor-probe`, look at the chunks whose `last` sits far above `p90`, and design the rule against all of them at once.

**(historic, for the record) The earlier dead end and why the first attempt could not measure it.** The rig reproduced the BASELINE on prod (`j1q9mjg8kewr2en`, 6 samples: max **94.4s**, mean −38.4s — same shape as the 113.5s ten-sample baseline), which only re-measures the OLD transcript; proving the fix needs a video transcribed by the NEW code. I picked the one video that could be transcribed with nothing to lose (`t91bnh582gnhgy8`, the 07-19 meeting, `transcriptionStatus` was NULL) and transcribed it — it worked, and it now has a transcript + AI summary. But it CANNOT measure drift: the rig's ground truth needs 20s clips loud enough to transcribe on their own, and this recording is quiet (whole file mean **−37.7 dB**, the middle 82–300s between **−53 and −66 dB**; the transcript legitimately covers only 0–82s and 300–416s). Amplifying it +25 dB did not help. **To prove the ≤15s target, one of the long LOUD videos must be re-transcribed (`fm3pz7yref328m7` 53 min, or `bbzt2424z6vvy6t` 117 min) — that OVERWRITES an existing transcript, so it is the owner's call, not mine.** Backups sit at `/root/backup-<videoId>-*.vtt` on the server, and the way HOME is now real, not hoped: `scripts/drift-restore.mjs` writes a VTT back and reads it straight back to confirm (proven on a scratch key — `wrote=79178 readBack=79178` — then the scratch object was deleted).

**A suspicion I raised and then RETRACTED, recorded so it is not re-raised:** the quiet recording plus a fluent transcript looked like an AI hallucination, and I nearly reported it as one. Two measurements killed it: the transcript's coverage gap matches exactly where the audio is silent, and the clip at 395s has real speech-band signal (mean −40.5 dB, peak −9.6 dB). The transcript is consistent with the audio. What IS real: **his meeting audio is quiet**, which is worth watching now that `TAB_AUDIO_GAIN=1.8` ships.

## ✅ THE 2-HOUR RECORDING QUESTION IS ANSWERED — SOAK PASSED (2026-08-04 02:19)

`VERDICT=PASS metric=inpage-full-contract filing=OK duration=OK(7193s of ~7200s) tone=OK sustained_ms=7200000` · analyzer `mean_440band_dB=-16.5 max_dB=-1.4 threshold=-50` · **179 multipart parts** · output `out-inpage/recording.bin` = **935,330,355 bytes (0.94 GB)**.

Read as the decision rule demanded — the RAW duration, not the harness's label: **7193 s of a 7200 s target, 7 seconds short over two hours.** The audio tone is present throughout, so this is not merely "a file was produced": the recording carries sound for its whole length, which is the part that matters for a meeting.

**What this proves:** the in-page recorder, the 200 ms chunk cadence (~36,000 messages), the service worker across a 2-hour lifetime, and the multipart upload all survive a full two hours continuously. The ranked risk list's top suspect (`inpage-recorder.ts:648`) is NOT implicated. Attempt 1's death at 28 minutes was environmental, now confirmed by a run that went 4.3x longer on the same code.

**What this does NOT prove, stated so it is not over-claimed:** it ran against the local mock server, not real Cloudflare R2 and not real transcription — so 2-hour *storage* and 2-hour *AI analysis* are still unproven. It also used a synthetic tone rather than speech. And 0.94 GB is the RAW recorder output at ~7.8 MB/min; the server-side compress step is what turns a 2-hour meeting into ~120 MB (measured 11.6:1 earlier tonight), so the storage answer depends on that step actually running — which is exactly the gap where 3 of 7 stored videos were found never to have been compressed.

**Reproduce:** `cd apps/browser-extension/e2e-audio-proof && PROOF_MINUTES=120 node run-proof-inpage.mjs` (after `pnpm build` in the extension). Free — no AI spend, no cloud writes. Do not have a dev server on port 3001.

## 🌓 DARK MODE NEEDED A RELOAD — REAL BUG, FIXED 2026-08-04 (`28398c7`)

**The owner reported it and he was right; I had already seen the symptom and explained it away.** In my own live check hours earlier the page reported `bodyClass: "light"` on a dark device with no cookie, and I concluded it was an artifact of the Browser pane applying its dark emulation after the script ran. That conclusion was wrong: the symptom was real, constant, and on every cold load.

**Measured cause on the live build:** `next/script strategy="beforeInteractive"` placed inside `<body>` emitted only `<link rel="preload" href="/theme-script.js">` into `<head>` and pushed the actual execution into the body via Next's deferred loader (`self.__next_s.push([...])`), which runs after hydration. First paint was therefore always light. A second visit ran it from cache early enough to look right — which is precisely why it read as intermittent instead of broken, and why a single check "passed".

**Fix (two halves, both needed):** `layout.tsx` reads the one source file at module scope and inlines it as a plain synchronous `<script>` in `<head>`; and `theme-script.js` now stamps the decided class onto `<html>` immediately, because inside `<head>` the `<body>` element does not exist yet and waiting for DOMContentLoaded would still paint light first. `<body>` is still stamped afterwards, since the theme toggle and the AI chat both read `document.body.classList`.

**Proof:** deleting the `<html>` line fails 2 of 8 assertions on the assertion itself; restoring gives 8/8. Suite 1219 passed / 0 failed. `tsc -b --force` 54s exit 0 — forced, because the cached run is a no-op (see gate hygiene above). Production `next build` exit 0 locally, so the module-scope file read is safe in the standalone output.

**Lesson kept on the board:** when a live check disagrees with the code, the code is not automatically right. I had a real signal and buried it under a plausible harness explanation; the owner's device settled it.

**Also answered for him:** Meetings already had day-grouping (Bugun / Shu hafta / Oldinroq), bulk select, delete, sharing, rename and pagination before last night — folders were the ONLY genuine gap and are now in. Search and sort exist in NEITHER section, so there is nothing to "port"; that would be new work.

**PENDING HIS ONE-WORD CONFIRM:** a full-screen toggle for the AI chat popup (proposed: an expand control next to the close button; tap to fill the screen, tap again to return). Not started.

## ✅ FULL LOGGED-IN WALKTHROUGH DONE (2026-08-04 21:20, prod `7af2cf3`)

The owner logged in himself in the Browser pane (he typed the password; I never saw it) and I walked the app from inside. **This closes the "I cannot verify behind login" gap that had been open all night.**

Verified by measurement, dark mode, viewport 1280x620 (the short window that reproduced the original bug):
- **Meetings folders are LIVE** — the "Yangi papka" button renders and folder cards appear.
- **Cost panel is no longer crushed** — `scrollHeight === clientHeight`, the exact failure the `shrink-0` fix targeted.
- **A/A font toggle is GONE**, `.ai-body` fixed at 16px.
- **Dark mode: 0 white blocks** on every screen visited.
- **Real visible overlaps: 0** across 57 static text elements.

**A false alarm I caught before reporting it, kept as method:** a naive overlap detector first returned 5 "overlaps". Four were the AI chat popup, which is `position: fixed` at max z-index and floats over the page BY DESIGN; the fifth was a comment scrolled out of its `overflow-y: auto` container — visible height **0**, clipped and invisible. Neither is a defect. Rule: an overlap detector must exclude floating layers and must measure the VISIBLE height inside the scroller, or it manufactures bugs.

**One REAL defect found in that walkthrough and fixed (`7af2cf3`):** the AI chat's welcome block was cut off — `clientHeight 73` vs `scrollHeight 96`, 23px of the greeting not rendered. Same `* { min-height: 0 }` family as `.ai-msg`, missed last night because the welcome only exists BEFORE the first question; there was no `.ai-welcome` rule at all. After deploy, re-measured: **96 / 96, not clipped**.

**STILL NOT VERIFIED — needs the owner's word:** replace-with-audio. It permanently destroys the video, so I will not press it on his data without him naming a video he does not need (or confirming he tested it himself).

## Archived 2026-08-13 — AI money/liveness stack, rounds 4-13 (full round-by-round log)

**ROUND 4 (`f0829c0`) — the ship gate caught the loose bound, and the loose bound was the COORDINATOR's spec, not the implementer's code.** The brief said "3 consecutive genuine non-quota errors" without bounding "genuine", so the streak counted 503s, socket resets and routine empty results as broken-key evidence — and because the counter was keyed by videoId while 4 retries × 3 concurrent chunks × 2 phases all fed it, three SIMULTANEOUS 503s reached the bound in about a second and put the whole job on full price with free quota untouched. It also never reset, so a later Retry began already tripped. Fixed: `isBrokenFreeKeyError()` matches ONLY the permanent auth family (400/401/403, `API_KEY_INVALID`, `PERMISSION_DENIED`), and `clearFreeTierStreak(videoId)` runs at both job starts. Also fixed in the same round: a FAILED silence probe no longer licenses paid gap-fill (`paidWorthy = speechSec != null && speechSec >= 60`), and **generation finally has a heartbeat** — extracted to a shared `lib/ai-job-heartbeat.ts` and the cron's generation query moved to `COALESCE(aiProgressAt, aiProcessingStartedAt, '')`, closing the case where a healthy 90-minute generation was duplicated into a second PAID run. Generalised to the brain (third shape in `feedback_unasserted_rule_is_not_a_rule`): a failure counter must count only the evidence it names, and you must check what concurrency/retries/phases share it.

**Gates at `f0829c0`, re-run by the main session: tsc 0 · web suite 1352 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** Stack: `56e1372` → `caa5409` → `251e97f` → `f0829c0`. Whitespace note, checked rather than assumed: the large `--shortstat` vs `-w` gap is entirely `docs/STATE.md` (my own line-ending churn), NOT app code.

**ROUND 5 (`5a6c156`) — the fourth ship gate caught two more, both created by round 4, and the fix was to DELETE rather than tune.** (a) The generation heartbeat introduced a duplicate-run bug: `aiProgressAt` is a SHARED cross-phase field — transcription stamps it and never clears it on success, generation never stamped it at all, and the heartbeat's first tick was at +5 min. So opening a video transcribed over an hour ago and pressing Generate left the row PROCESSING with a day-old marker, the cron read it as instantly stale, and (unlike transcription) the generation loop has no JS freshness re-check, so it started a SECOND concurrent paid generation. Fixed: both generation claims now stamp `aiProgressAt`, and the heartbeat gained a leading stamp. (b) My own instruction to reset the streak at job start re-opened the round-11 deadlock for SHORT videos — the single-chunk summary path can only ever record ONE signal against a bound of three, and the reset zeroed it on every retry, so a revoked key errored those videos forever with a valid paid key unused.

**The correcting insight, and it is the shape that has worked every time in this area: the counter was only ever needed because the trigger used to include transients. It no longer does — `isBrokenFreeKeyError` matches ONLY the permanent auth family, and an auth failure is by definition not transient, so ONE full-chain auth failure is already conclusive.** The whole streak mechanism is therefore deleted: `free-tier-health` is now a per-job verdict Set, one auth failure marks the key unusable for that job and the same call continues on paid, and the job-start clear now HELPS (a retry after the key is fixed re-probes free once) instead of defeating a bound. **The classifier, not a counter, is what protects the money** — the pins proving a 503 burst, socket errors and empty output produce ZERO paid calls stayed green.

**Gates at `5a6c156`, re-run by the main session: tsc 0 · web suite 1351 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** Code-only against production (`apps` + `packages`, excluding docs): **42 files, +899 / −1460 — 561 fewer lines than production.** Stack: `56e1372` → `caa5409` → `251e97f` → `f0829c0` → `5a6c156`. **Five blind reviews so far; every single one found a real money or liveness defect, and every one of those was introduced by the previous round's fix** — which is why nothing here ships on a green suite alone.

**ROUND 6 (`aaec4ef`) — the classifier itself was too broad, and deleting the counter is what exposed it.** The fifth ship gate proved `isBrokenFreeKeyError` matched Gemini's NON-auth `HTTP 400` (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` — "Request contains an invalid argument"), and that a bare digit arm even matched a `JSON.parse` error reading *"Unterminated string in JSON at position 400"*. With the counter gone, one such misread billed an ENTIRE job on paid with the free tier untouched. Also found: `workflows/generate-ai.ts:1092` did not classify quota first, unlike the other two call sites. Fixed: `HTTP 401|403` stays broad; `400` counts only alongside an explicit auth token (`API_KEY_INVALID` / "API key not valid" / `PERMISSION_DENIED` / `UNAUTHENTICATED`); the bare digit arm is deleted; all three sites now test quota before auth. Four pins written with the REAL message strings — the invalid-argument 400, the JSON offset-400 SyntaxError, a mixed quota+transient set, and a genuine 401 that must still mark the job.

**⭐ THE COORDINATOR-LEVEL LESSON, recorded because it caused three of these rounds: `feedback_unasserted_rule_is_not_a_rule` now carries a fourth shape — before deleting a safety mechanism because "it is no longer needed", PROVE the thing that made it necessary is gone, with the actual inputs.** The counter existed to absorb classifier false positives; I ordered it deleted on the reasoning that the classifier had become precise, and never measured that it had. "Deletion has worked before in this area" is a bias, not evidence.

**Gates at `aaec4ef`, re-run by the main session: tsc 0 · web suite 1356 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** Code-only against production: **42 files, +1007 / −1460**. Stack: `56e1372` → `caa5409` → `251e97f` → `f0829c0` → `5a6c156` → `aaec4ef`.

**ROUND 7 (`8111311`) — the sixth ship gate found the narrowing had made a revoked key INVISIBLE on the whole transcription path, and the fix belonged upstream of the classifier.** `transcribeWithGemini` always goes through the Files API first, so with a dead free key the FIRST error of every job comes from `startUploadSession` — and that throw site built its message **without the response body**: `Gemini upload init failed (HTTP 400)`, with no auth token for the classifier to match. Measured: that string classifies false, while `…(HTTP 400): API key not valid…` classifies true. Consequence: the rethrow was swallowed as a gap, every chunk failed identically, and a video finished with an empty/gap-only transcript while the paid key was never touched and Retry reproduced it forever.

**The fix deliberately did NOT widen the regex — that oscillation had already been paid for twice. The evidence was being thrown away at the source, so the source was fixed:** all four upload legs (`gemini-transcribe.ts` init + finalize, and the same pair in `gemini-batch-transcribe.ts`) now append the response body, truncated to 300 chars, keeping the existing prefix. Second finding, same class: `assertFreeAiResultUsable` runs `JSON.parse` on the MODEL'S OWN CONTENT, and V8 quotes the offending input into the message — so a reply beginning `HTTP 403 …` read as a revoked key and billed the job. Fixed structurally rather than with another regex exclusion: those throws carry a `Symbol.for` tag and the classifier returns false for tagged errors before any regex runs.

**⭐ THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THIS ROUND IS THE TEST, not the fix.** Every earlier pin mocked `transcribeWithGemini`, so every string they asserted was authored at the test boundary — none was a string the code could actually produce. That is precisely why **1356 green tests could not see the hole**. The new pin drives the REAL `transcribeWithGemini` with a stubbed global `fetch` returning Google's genuine invalid-key body, and carries the discriminating negative (a non-auth 400 body must still classify false).

**Gates at `8111311`, re-run by the main session: tsc 0 · web suite 1359 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** Stack: `56e1372` → `caa5409` → `251e97f` → `f0829c0` → `5a6c156` → `aaec4ef` → `8111311`. **Seven blind reviews; every one found a real money or liveness defect, and every one of those was created by the previous round's fix.** The convergence signal is that the rounds are shrinking — 36 files, then 13, 10, 4, 6 — and the fixes have moved from tuning a value to correcting the source.

**⚠️ NEW RISK INTRODUCED THIS ROUND, under review at the time of writing:** appending a response body to an error message is new attack surface — error bodies can echo request context, and these messages travel into logs, `transcriptionError` in the database, and ops alerts. Whether an API key or a signed URL can now appear in any of them is the first question of the current ship gate. Do not deploy until that is answered.

**ROUND 8 (`7d3ecd4`) — the same defect, one classifier to the left.** The seventh ship gate found the `Symbol.for` "unusable model output" tag guarded `isBrokenFreeKeyError` but NOT `isQuotaLikeAiError` — and quota is evaluated FIRST and also opens the paid path. Proven on this repo's Node: a free model returning the content `"Rate limit reached, try later"` produces `Unexpected token 'R', "Rate limit"... is not valid JSON`, which reads as quota-like; so a genuine 429 on model A plus unparseable content on model B was read as "free tier exhausted" and billed the whole summary on paid with model B's free quota intact. Round-14's "position 400" defect verbatim, fixed on one arm and left on the other.

**⭐ THE PATTERN, and the instruction that finally addressed it: every review pointed at ONE site, every fix repaired THAT site, and the next review found the same defect one file over.** So round 8 was told to fix the class, not the lines — and to prove the sweep. It did: `isQuotaLikeAiError` now rejects tagged errors before any regex; `isDeadlineError` is structural (`instanceof` / `name === "AbortError"`) and cannot collide; and a grep across `lib` + `workflows` shows the only other message regexes are the sanitizer itself, the retry check (liveness-only), a speaker-label filter and a URL helper — none paid-opening.

**Two smaller items from the same gate, both fixed:** Google's raw response body was being persisted into `videos.metadata.transcriptionError.message` and served to **every share-page viewer, public links included** — no credential leak (the key travels in the URL, and Google's JSON envelopes never echo the request URI; only the GFE HTML page does, and it exceeds the truncation), but upstream detail shown to strangers with only a truncation length holding the line. Now `lib/ai-error-message.ts` keeps the full body in the LOG and persists a sanitized reason. And `withGeminiRetry`'s transient regex `/429|500|501|503/` had no word boundaries, so a permanent 403 whose body carried a project number like `763029500123` looked transient and burned backoff — bounded now.

**Gates at `7d3ecd4`, re-run by the main session: tsc 0 · web suite 1367 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** Code-only against production: **50 files, +1360 / −1468.** Stack: `56e1372` → `caa5409` → `251e97f` → `f0829c0` → `5a6c156` → `aaec4ef` → `8111311` → `7d3ecd4`. **Eight blind reviews, eight real defects, every one created by the previous fix** — the count is the honest measure of how narrow this area is, not a reason to stop reviewing.

**⚠️ THE QUESTION THE CURRENT GATE MUST ANSWER FIRST:** the new sanitizer deliberately DROPS the response body — and round 7's whole fix was that upload-leg errors now CARRY that body so the auth classifier can see `API_KEY_INVALID`. If the sanitized message reaches any classifier, round 7 is silently undone and a revoked key is invisible again. Ordering at every persist-and-classify site is the first thing to verify; do not deploy before it is answered.

**✅ SHIPPED AND LIVE 2026-08-13: `7d3ecd4` is running on the team's server.** The eighth ship gate returned **SHIP** — no money defect, no path by which a revoked key becomes invisible, no regression against `5c9a52d`. Verified INSIDE the running container rather than by its existence: the old mode-chooser strings (`aiModeCheap`/`aiModeFast`/`aiChooseMode`) are **absent**, the `continue-paid` route is gone, `aiSpeedMode` plumbing is **absent** from every built `.js`, and the new sentence is present verbatim — *"Avval bepul AI limitidan foydalanadi; u tugasa, kutmasdan darhol pullik yo'lda davom etadi."* From the public internet: `/api/health` 200 with valid TLS and `db:ok storage:ok`, share page 200, login 200; `data365dashboard.uz` still answers throughout. **Rollback is one command — `data365-web:5c9a52d` is still on the host.**

**ROUND 9 (`1d85710`) — the two non-blocking gaps the ship gate handed forward, folded in.** The sanitizer's prefix arm was ANCHORED, so a wrapped message (`Transcription quota/credits exhausted for <id>: …`) missed it and fell to a 200-char slice that carried ~95 chars of Google's JSON body into `videos.metadata.transcriptionError.message` — which `ShareVideo` ships in the RSC payload of a PUBLIC share link. Now lazy (`^[\s\S]*?\(HTTP \d+\)`), keeping the wrapper context and stopping at the first status marker. And `lib/generate-ai.ts`'s `aiGenerationError` — a sibling persist site the earlier sweep missed — now routes through the same sanitizer. **The sweep was demanded with evidence this time and delivered: the five writers that put anything into client-visible `videos.metadata` are listed, three are static strings, and both dynamic ones now share one sanitizer.** Gates: tsc 0 · **1370 passed / 26 skipped** · real `next build` 0 · 5 files +62/−11, `-w` identical. **NOT deployed** — under its own short blind review.

**THE HONEST TALLY FOR THIS FEATURE: 9 build rounds, 9 blind reviews, 9 real defects — every one created by the previous round's fix, and green gates caught none of them.** The recurring shape, now recorded in the brain (`feedback_unasserted_rule_is_not_a_rule`, four shapes): a review names ONE site, the fix repairs THAT site, and the next review finds the same defect one file over. What finally broke the cycle was instructing the implementer to fix the CLASS and prove the sweep — and the single most valuable artefact produced was not a fix but a test that drives the REAL code path with a stubbed `fetch`, because every earlier pin asserted strings authored at the test boundary, which is exactly why 1356 green tests could not see a live hole.

**✅ ROUND 9 (`1d85710`) ALSO SHIPPED AND LIVE-VERIFIED.** Its blind review returned **NONE / SHIP** — the first completely clean review of this stack. The reviewer ran the real sanitizer against 11 shapes and Google's body survived in zero producible cases; ordering confirmed safe (both call sites are terminal persist sites, every classifier runs earlier on the raw error object, and no reader keys off `.message`, so round 7's `API_KEY_INVALID` detection is untouched). Deployed to the team's server: image `data365-web:1d85710`, `/api/health` 200 with valid TLS and `db:ok storage:ok`, share page 200, login 200, `data365dashboard.uz` unaffected. **Rollback stays one command — `5c9a52d` and `7d3ecd4` are both still on the host.**

**LIVE END-TO-END CHECK AFTER THE DEPLOY: 22 of 23 videos open and stream. The 23rd is NOT a regression — and finding it was worth the check.** `r5m2y8jxhatkmef` ("IMG_1400", created 2026-08-12 21:54) has `transcriptionStatus NULL` and an upload row in phase `error` with **uploaded 0 / total 0** — the upload failed at the very first byte, so the share page renders from the DB row while the media 404s. It is the only failed upload in the table.
- **The important part: `processing_error` and `processing_message` are both NULL.** The system recorded NOTHING about why it failed, so the owner had no way to know his upload never landed. That is precisely the gap the round in flight is closing — and it also shows why the persist-site work matters beyond privacy: the field has to be both SAFE and USEFUL.
- **OWNER-VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE:** one of his uploads from last night silently did not arrive. He will want to re-upload `IMG_1400`.

**🔒 NEW ROUND IN FLIGHT, more sensitive than anything in the last stack.** The final gate flagged `lib/video-processing.ts:30`: `setVideoProcessingError` writes a RAW `err.message` into `videoUploads.processingError`, and that field reaches the share page at `ProgressCircle.tsx:183`. These are upload/ffmpeg/S3 errors, and **an AWS SDK error can carry a presigned URL** — not upstream detail like a project number, but a working access credential for the object, printed where anyone with the share link can read it. Pre-existing, not caused by this stack. The fix is specified as an ALLOWLIST (the shape already used at `workflows/transcribe.ts:372`) rather than another sanitizer, plus a sweep across EVERY table and response that can carry a raw error to a client — the last sweep was scoped to `videos.metadata` and this site sat one table over, which is the fourth time that shape has appeared.

**ROUND 10 (`96b3723`) — the presigned-URL exposure, fixed as an ALLOWLIST.** `lib/processing-error.ts` → `persistableProcessingError()` persists only the MATCHED TOKEN of a known failure family (`Mux failed: NNN`, `fetch failed`, manifest-not-found, timeouts, `SERVER_BUSY`, capacity, `Application failed to respond`) or the literal `"failed"` — returning the match rather than the input is what makes a URL unable to ride along even when a known token co-occurs with one. All FIVE writers wired (`video-processing`, `process-video`, `import-loom-video`, `finalize-desktop-recording`, `admin-reprocess-video`), enforced by a source-scan pin; full detail goes to a log. Gates: tsc 0 · **1374 passed / 26 skipped** · real `next build` 0. NOT deployed — under blind review.

**⭐ MEASURED ON PRODUCTION WHILE THAT REVIEW RAN — two answers the reviewer could not get, because it has no database access:**
1. **No exposure exists today.** Rows in `video_uploads` with any `processing_error` at all: **0**. Rows containing `http` or `X-Amz`: **0**. Same check across `videos.metadata.transcriptionError.message` and `aiGenerationError`: **0**. So this whole persist-site effort is PREVENTIVE, not remedial — nothing needs cleaning up, and no legacy row can render a credential to a share-page viewer.
2. **But the same measurement exposes a bigger gap than the one being fixed: that field has NEVER been written.** `processing_error` is NULL in every row — **including `r5m2y8jxhatkmef` ("IMG_1400"), the upload that really did fail last night at 0 bytes.** So `setVideoProcessingError` is evidently not reached on the upload-abort path at all. Making the field safe does not make it useful; the owner still learns nothing when an upload silently fails. **The next round's real question is not "is the allowlist tight enough" but "why is nothing written when an upload dies, and what should the user see?"** — hold this until the current gate answers whether the allowlist is safe-but-silent, then act on both together.

**ROUND 11 (`712d8f9`) — the gate on the allowlist returned SHIP, with two findings, and one of ITS findings was wrong.** The security property is proven: the reviewer ran the real module through 15 adversarial inputs and only the bare token ever survived — a presigned URL containing `SERVER_BUSY` in its path yields `"SERVER_BUSY"`, `fetch failed to https://…X-Amz-Signature=…` yields `"fetch failed"`.
- **The review's finding 1 — "legacy rows still leak, run an `UPDATE` on production" — is FALSE, and this is worth recording as a method note: it reasoned from code with no database access, while I measured the database.** Rows with any `processing_error`: **0**; containing `http` or `X-Amz`: **0**; same across the two `videos.metadata` error fields: **0**. No cleanup was run and none was needed. A reviewer's reach ends where the data begins.
- **Finding 2 was real and was a UX downgrade the fix itself introduced:** `"failed"` is truthy, so it SUPPRESSED `t("progressProcessingFailedOwner")` and showed a bare English word where the Uzbek sentence had told the viewer what to do. Unknown errors now persist as `""` — falsy, so the localized copy returns. Identical security property, one character.
- **Finding 3 is the one that actually matters to the owner, and TWO INDEPENDENT METHODS AGREED ON IT** — the reviewer found it in the code, I found it in production. The real upload-abort path is `actions/video/mark-upload-failed.ts` and the reconcile-storage stale sweep, **neither of which was among the five writers patched in round 10**; both wrote a bare `{ phase: "error" }`. That is exactly why "IMG_1400" has both columns NULL. They now set a static `processingMessage: "Uploading"` plus an allowlisted token (`"Upload interrupted"` / `"Upload stalled"`), with a source-scan pin that fails if either file ever ships a bare error phase again.

**Gates at `712d8f9`: tsc 0 · web suite 1375 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0 · 4 files +51/−6, `-w` identical.** NOT deployed — under its own blind review. **This was the FOURTH time a sweep in this stack was scoped too narrowly** (five writers patched, two real ones missed), which is the recurring shape recorded in `feedback_unasserted_rule_is_not_a_rule`.

**🧹 DEBT, deliberately not paid mid-flight: this board is far past its size budget** (~260 KB) and reloads in full every session. It needs the main-session compression pass — a ~15-line status block at the top plus MOVING the older round logs to `docs/STATE_ARCHIVE.md`. Do it at the next natural pause, in the main session only, never delegated.

**ROUNDS 12-13 (`07ed752`, `7a7546b`) — the share-page failure copy, and a root cause worth remembering.** Round 12's gate found the round-11 tokens were TRUTHY, so `errorMessage || t(...)` rendered bare English (`Upload interrupted`) where the localized actionable Uzbek sentence used to show — fixing the rare unknown case and breaking the most common one, untranslated. Round 12 introduced a single mapper (`processing-failure-message.ts`) translating known tokens to new uz/ru/en keys, with everything else — empty, ops-only tokens, legacy raw rows — falling back to the localized default; and made the reconcile sweep derive its message from each row's ACTUAL phase with `COALESCE`, instead of claiming "Uploading" for rows that had failed at a later stage.

**Round 13's gate then found the structural cause, and it is the cleanest lesson of the whole stack: `errorMessage` was carrying TWO KINDS OF VALUE** — stored DB tokens from one producer, and already-localized sentences from `getStalledProcessingMessage` in another. Routing both through a token mapper discarded the sentences, so a merely-QUEUED video told its owner "Processing failed." with no instruction, replacing "Video processing stalled. Retry processing." Fixed by removing the overloading rather than special-casing it: the stall branch now emits a KEY, its `t` parameter is gone, and the invariant is stated at both ends — **everything entering that field is a token or key the mapper translates; nothing pre-localized, nothing raw.** Generalised to the brain under `reference_software_design_ousterhout`: if you cannot name a field's type in one honest word, it is two fields wearing one name, and a per-producer guard clause would only have broken again at the next producer.

**Also from round 13, the third occurrence of the same testing flaw: the pin sat at the wrong boundary** — it asserted the helper's return value in isolation and never the render, which is exactly why 1381 green tests could not see a live regression. The new pin drives a stalled row THROUGH the render path with the real uz locale and asserts both the owner and the anonymous viewer receive the instruction.

**Gates at `7a7546b`: tsc 0 · web suite 1382 passed / 26 skipped · real `next build` 0.** NOT deployed — under its own blind review. **Production is still `1d85710`, which is reviewed, live and free of every defect found in rounds 10-13.**

**THE NEXT ACTION:** read the final blind review of `5c9a52d..251e97f` (in flight when this was written). If it returns SHIP → push `qa-fixes`, trigger the Coolify deploy on the NEW host, then verify by the `docker ps` image tag against `git rev-parse` before believing anything landed. If it returns DO-NOT-SHIP → fold its findings first; this stack has already had one blocker caught that way (a revoked free key would have deadlocked every analysis).

**BLOCKERS / OWNER-ONLY:** a real record→upload→appears-on-site run with the browser extension (needs a logged-in browser, which no session can do) · the PAID Gemini key is still missing — the key he supplied is the FREE one, so once the daily free allowance is spent the analysis stops instead of continuing on paid · the new server's root password still needs rotating, it passed through chat.

## ▶ RESUME HERE — 2026-08-18 ~08:00 (history — superseded by the block above)

**THE OWNER'S LIVE COMPLAINT, 2026-08-18:** *"nega uploaddan keyin ham processing deb turibdi va ai tahlil ishlamayapti mobiledan"* — and it is a REAL defect, root-caused with evidence, not a stale page.

**ROOT CAUSE: transcription and AI are started by the BROWSER, and nothing on the server rescues an upload whose browser went away.**
`useImportUpload.ts:254-259` awaits `triggerVideoProcessing` client-side after the bytes land; `/api/videos/[videoId]/generate/route.ts:171-172` is a client `fetch`; `lib/transcribe.ts:74` is the only place that sets `PROCESSING`. Background the tab — a phone locking its screen — and the call never fires. The video then sits at `transcriptionStatus = NULL` **forever**: `recover-stale-ai-jobs` only re-kicks work that already carries an `aiStartPendingUpload` marker, and a kickoff that never reached the server leaves no marker. `reconcile-storage` and `poll-batch-jobs` are not even scheduled on the host.

**▶ WHERE THIS STANDS RIGHT NOW (2026-08-18).** Root cause found and confirmed by an independent review; the fix is split into two waves so the owner gets relief first. **✅ WAVE 1 IS DEPLOYED — production runs `data365-web:f917459` since 2026-08-18 12:17 CEST.** Live-verified after the switch: `/api/health` 200 `db:ok storage:ok` · `/login` 200 · a share page 200 · the team's other site still answering · the only log line is the known benign duplicate-column migration notice. **The new code is proven to be IN the running image, not just in the tarball** — `grep "raw upload kept"` finds it inside the container's built output, with `"Video conversion timed out"` used as the control that the grep method works at all. Rollback is one tag change; `/opt/data365/docker-compose.app.yml.bak-f917459` holds the previous file and every earlier image is still on the host.

**⚠️ THE LIVE NUMBER IS NOT YET MEASURED, and cannot be measured from this machine.** The metric — time from `multipart/complete` to `processingStartedAt`, target under 3 minutes against a 2 h 03 m baseline — needs one real upload. next-auth here uses **JWT sessions** (`sessions` table has 0 rows), so there is no session row to mint and no login credential on this machine; forging a token with the app secret is not an acceptable route. **So: ask the owner (or anyone on the team) to upload one video, then read the two timestamps.** Do NOT record wave 1 as verified before that number exists — the unit tests and the artifact check prove the code shipped, not that the wait is gone.

(superseded) **Wave 1 was IMPLEMENTED and BEING DEPLOYED — `f9970b5` + `f917459` (the two holes the blind review found: an ungated compress after a genuine copy FAILURE, and a COMPLETE minted after gap-fill for a deleted video). Image `data365-web:f917459` building on the host; the owner approved deploying now. Not pushed; `pnpm typecheck` verified 0 errors from the repo root by the main session, not taken on the author's word. Blind non-author review of the diff is running. Spec: `docs/plans/2026-08-18-wave1-SPEC.md`, evidence: `docs/plans/2026-08-18-stuck-processing-SCOPE.md`.** Wave 1 = (1) a copy TIMEOUT must not buy a 45-min re-encode, and the copy budget scales with duration; (2) transcription must not queue behind optimization, and `startPendingAiRequest` must filter `deletedAt`; (3) cancel-on-delete as a FatalError checked in the chunk LOOP, never inside the per-chunk catch at `transcribe.ts:1192-1204` which swallows throws. Six RED-first tests, three gates. **Wave 2, not started, do not lose it:** progress for the remaining long stage (carrier = `video_uploads.processing_progress` during remux; extraction counters in `videos.metadata` are second), the re-press toast + `keepalive` on the press (server already answers `alreadyRunning`/`queued`, `GenerateAiPanel.tsx:181-186` throws the body away), and the stranded-upload rescue inside `recover-stale-ai-jobs` — with a plain metadata attempts counter, NOT the `uploadRescueAttempts` column the first draft assumed (it does not exist). The review also deleted the proposed ffmpeg semaphore as speculative.

**Review findings worth keeping even if the code changes:** optimization is NOT pure cost — compress is genuinely needed for VP9/Opus webm → H.264/AAC on iOS/Safari (`video-convert.ts:196-198`) and for >1080p/>2.5 Mbps downscale (`:205-211`); it is only the *timeout fallthrough* and the *unbounded duration* that are wrong. The raw upload really is served when `result.mp4` is absent (`app/api/playlist/route.ts:286-297`). A user who has NOT pressed AI gets no status poll before the claim, so wave 2's remux progress must ride the upload-progress UI — **resolved:** `useUploadProgress` is exported from `app/s/[videoId]/_components/ProgressCircle.tsx` and consumed by the dashboard card at `app/(org)/dashboard/caps/components/CapCard/CapCard.tsx:297`, so the card already polls `video_uploads` and will show remux progress once it is written.

**🔴 ROOT CAUSE, found in the container logs 2026-08-18 — this is the answer, everything above it is the trail that led here.** All three of his attempts logged the identical line: `[process-video] Optimization failed for <id>, keeping the original raw upload playable: Error: Video conversion timed out`. `workflows/process-video.ts:113` calls `optimizeRemoteVideoToMp4(videoUrl)` with no options, so `lib/video-convert.ts` applies `DEFAULT_COPY_TIMEOUT_MS = 10 min` (`:20`) then `DEFAULT_COMPRESS_TIMEOUT_MS = 45 min` (`:23`) — **up to 55 minutes of ffmpeg that cannot finish**, plus pulling a multi-GB file down to feed it, before the catch at `:127` gives up and transcription finally starts. Transcription itself then took 10 minutes. The product already works without the optimized copy — that fallback is what runs today. **Fix, re-ranked: (1) probe first and skip a conversion that cannot finish (`probeRemoteVideo` already exists and is already called in the catch at `:135`); (2) stop transcription queueing behind optimization; (3) progress for whatever long stage remains; (4) cancel-on-delete; (5) re-press toast; (6) stranded rescue.** Full evidence + the design under blind review: `docs/plans/2026-08-18-stuck-processing-SCOPE.md`.

**⛔ SUPERSEDED SAME DAY — read `docs/plans/2026-08-18-stuck-processing-SCOPE.md` before acting on the paragraph above.** The nginx log refuted the "lost trigger" story: that evening he was on a phone and he DID press the button — six times, every call 200. The hours went into the stage BEFORE the first AI call (fetch object → ffmpeg extract → chunk), which reports no progress at all: measured 2h10m for a 126-min video where a same-length one took 24 min, while the AI itself takes 2–8 min. A deleted video is still being written to (two were written 9 and 44 min after deletion) — but the stronger "his retries had the box chewing 3–4 copies at once" claim was CHECKED AND NOT PROVEN: all three abandoned attempts have zero `transcript_chunks` and zero `ai_usage_events`, so nothing heavy is on record for them. Treat concurrency as an open question, not a finding. **What IS conclusive: `deletedAt` appears nowhere in `workflows/transcribe.ts`, `workflows/process-video.ts`, `lib/transcribe.ts` or `lib/generate-ai.ts` — no stage ever asks whether the video still exists, so a started job runs to the end, paid API calls included, after the user trashes it.** Real fix set, in order: (1) show the silent stage, (2) cancel on delete, (3) no silent re-press, (4) stranded-upload rescue bound by the manual-AI decision. Metric: 23/32 attempts COMPLETE in 30 days = **72%**, target ≥95%, plus "first visible progress within 60 s of the press".

**Evidence (prod, not inferred):** 4 videos frozen at `transcriptionStatus = NULL` since 18/30 July, never recovered · on 2026-08-17 17:36→18:11 the owner uploaded ONE file four times, three froze (one left a `video_uploads` row `multipart/uploading/0 of 0` that never advanced) and the fourth worked. Storage is NOT to blame: an R2 browser preflight still returns 204 with the right origin, so the 08-14 CORS cause has not regressed. Full write-up + the metric: **`docs/plans/2026-08-18-stuck-processing-SCOPE.md`**.

**METRIC (a query, not an opinion):** non-deleted videos with `transcriptionStatus` NULL or PROCESSING older than 1h. Baseline **4 → 0** today (the four July strays were trashed on the owner's instruction; recoverable for 7 days). The fix must hold it at 0, and a tab killed the instant the bytes land must still reach COMPLETE within 15 min.

**OWNER'S ORDER, 2026-08-18:** *"Xafa qilib tuzat, muammo bo'lmasin boshqa. Telefonda ham, kompyuterda ham."* → build BOTH layers: (A) a scheduled server-side rescue that starts a stranded upload itself, idempotent and double-spend-safe; (B) a client kickoff that survives a backgrounded mobile tab. Not started as of this line — design was out with the architect.

The mock now carries `push` + `refresh` (grep-verified: the only two router methods the reachable chain calls), plus ONE assertion pinning that the refresh happens — mutation-checked, i.e. removing the component's `router.refresh()` makes the new test fail.
